# List 9

## Unit 1

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>entangle</b></td><td>/ɪnˈtæŋɡl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使缠住，使卷入；使复杂</td><td>entangle the negotiations<br>使谈判变复杂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 New objections entangled the negotiations and delayed the deal for weeks.  新的反对意见使谈判陷入纠缠，把交易推迟了数周。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 complexify</del></td><td>/kəmˈplɛksəfaɪ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使复杂化</td><td>complexify a simple task<br>把简单任务复杂化</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 New regulations only complexify an already confusing process.  新规定只是让本已混乱的流程更加复杂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 perplex</td><td>/pərˈpleks/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使困惑，使费解</td><td>perplex the researchers<br>使研究者困惑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The strange results perplexed the researchers for several long months.  奇怪的结果让研究者困惑了好几个月。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sophisticate</td><td>/səˈfɪstəˌkeɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>使复杂化；老于世故者</td><td>sophisticate the design further<br>把设计进一步复杂化</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Engineers sophisticated the model until nobody could explain it.  工程师把模型改得越来越复杂，最后没人能解释清楚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 simplify</td><td>/ˈsɪmpləˌfaɪ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>简化，使简明</td><td>simplify the application form<br>简化申请表</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new software simplifies the way teachers record grades.  新软件简化了老师登记成绩的方式。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 streamline</td><td>/ˈstriːmlaɪn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>精简，使效率提高</td><td>streamline the approval process<br>精简审批流程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The hospital streamlined admissions and cut waiting times in half.  医院精简了入院手续，把等候时间减半。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>episodic</b></td><td>/ˌepɪˈsɑːdɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>间歇性的；不定期的</td><td>episodic bursts of rain<br>断断续续的阵雨</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Episodic bursts of rain kept interrupting the outdoor ceremony all afternoon.  断断续续的阵雨整个下午不断打断露天典礼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 aperiodic</td><td>/ˌeɪpɪriˈɑdɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>非周期的，无规律的</td><td>aperiodic seismic tremors<br>无规律的地震颤动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The signal turned out to be aperiodic and hard to model.  这个信号最终被证明是非周期的，难以建模。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 discontinuous</td><td>/ˌdɪskənˈtɪnjuəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不连续的，间断的</td><td>a discontinuous record<br>不连续的记录</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The fossil record here is discontinuous and full of long gaps.  这里的化石记录是间断的，有很多长期空缺。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fitful</td><td>/ˈfɪtfl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>断断续续的</td><td>a fitful night's sleep<br>断断续续的一夜睡眠</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Noise from the street gave her a fitful night's sleep.  街上的噪音让她一夜睡得断断续续。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 intermittent</td><td>/ˌɪntərˈmɪtənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>断断续续的</td><td>intermittent power failures<br>断断续续的停电</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Intermittent power failures made it impossible to finish the experiment that night.  断断续续的停电使当晚无法完成那项实验。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sporadic</td><td>/spəˈrædɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>断断续续的</td><td>sporadic gunfire<br>断续的枪声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Sporadic gunfire continued through the night on the edge of town.  镇子边缘整夜断断续续传来枪声。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 constant</td><td>/ˈkɑːnstənt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>不变的，恒定的；忠诚的</td><td>a constant friend<br>忠诚不变的朋友</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her support remained constant through every difficult year.  她的支持在每个艰难年头都不曾改变。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 continuous</td><td>/kənˈtɪnjuːəs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>连续的，不间断的</td><td>a continuous line<br>一条连续的线</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The machine has been in continuous operation for six days.  这台机器已连续运转六天。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 habitual</td><td>/həˈbɪtʃuəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>习惯性的，惯常的</td><td>his habitual morning walk<br>他惯常的晨间散步</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Habitual lateness eventually cost him the job.  惯常迟到最终让他失去了工作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 periodic</td><td>/ˌpɪriˈɑːdɪk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>周期性的，定期的</td><td>periodic safety inspections<br>定期安全检查</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The bridge undergoes periodic checks every six months.  这座桥每六个月接受一次定期检查。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 steady</td><td>/ˈstedi/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>稳定的，持续不变的</td><td>a steady stream of visitors<br>络绎不绝的来访者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Sales have shown steady growth for eight straight quarters.  销售额已连续八个季度稳定增长。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>entice</b></td><td>/ɪnˈtaɪs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>诱使，引诱</td><td>entice customers with discounts<br>用折扣吸引顾客</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Free samples were used to entice shoppers into the new store.  免费样品被用来引诱购物者进入新店。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 allure</td><td>/əˈlʊr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>吸引，诱惑</td><td>the allure of fame<br>名声的诱惑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cheap rents allure young artists to this run-down district.  低廉的租金吸引年轻艺术家来到这个破旧街区。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bait</td><td>/beɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>故意惹恼，戏弄；引诱</td><td>bait him about his accent<br>拿他的口音戏弄他</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The older boys baited him about his accent until he cried.  大孩子们拿他的口音戏弄他，直到他哭了出来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 beguile</td><td>/bɪˈɡaɪl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>欺骗，诱骗；使着迷</td><td>beguile the audience<br>令观众着迷</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The magician could beguile an audience for a full hour.  这位魔术师能让观众着迷整整一小时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 decoy</td><td>/ˈdiːkɔɪ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>诱饵，圈套；诱骗</td><td>act as a decoy<br>充当诱饵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The wooden duck served as a decoy to attract real birds.  木鸭子被用作诱饵来吸引真鸟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 seduce</td><td>/sɪˈduːs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>诱使（背离正道），引诱</td><td>seduce someone into crime<br>引诱某人犯罪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 High salaries seduced many teachers away from the classroom.  高薪诱使许多教师离开了课堂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 solicit</td><td>/səˈlɪsɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>请求，征求；招揽</td><td>solicit donations<br>募集捐款</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The charity solicited funds from local businesses all winter.  该慈善机构整个冬天向本地企业募捐。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tempt</td><td>/ˈtempt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>诱惑，引诱</td><td>tempt fate<br>冒险行事，玩火</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The smell of fresh bread tempted us into the little bakery.  新鲜面包的香味把我们诱进了那家小面包店。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lead on</td><td>/liːd ˈɑːn/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>引诱，误导（使抱幻想）</td><td>lead someone on<br>误导某人抱幻想</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She finally realized he was leading her on with empty promises.  她终于意识到他在用空头承诺吊着她。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 formidable</td><td>/ˈfɔːrmɪdəbl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>难以对付的，令人敬畏的</td><td>a formidable opponent<br>难以对付的对手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She faced a formidable opponent in the final round.  她在最后一轮遇到了难以对付的对手。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>entrance</b></td><td>/ɪnˈtræns/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使入迷</td><td>entrance the small audience<br>使小小的观众入迷</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The puppet show entranced the small audience of restless children completely.  木偶戏让这群坐不住的小观众完全入了迷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 enrapture</del></td><td>/ɪnˈræptʃər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使狂喜，使着迷</td><td>enrapture the whole audience<br>使全场观众入迷</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The young pianist enraptured everyone in the small concert hall.  这位年轻钢琴家让小音乐厅里的每个人都入了迷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 enthrall</td><td>/ɪnˈθrɔːl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>（像用魔咒般）吸引</td><td>enthrall the entire audience<br>吸引住全场观众</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The magician enthralled the entire audience with a single deck of cards.  魔术师仅用一副牌就吸引住了全场观众。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ravish</td><td>/ˈrævɪʃ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使陶醉，使沉迷</td><td>ravish the eye with color<br>用色彩令人陶醉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The painter's bold colors ravish the eye and hold it captive.  这位画家大胆的色彩令人赏心悦目、目不转睛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 transport</td><td>/trænsˈpɔːrt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>运输，运送</td><td>transport the goods<br>运送货物</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Trucks transport fresh produce to the city every morning.  卡车每天早晨把新鲜农产品运往城市。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 carry away</td><td>/ˈkæri əˈweɪ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>使忘形，使激动不已</td><td>get carried away by excitement<br>兴奋得忘乎所以</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He got carried away and promised far more than he could deliver.  他一激动忘了形，承诺远超他能做到的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 bore</td><td>/bɔːr/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>令人厌烦的人或事物</td><td>an unbearable old bore<br>让人受不了的老唠叨鬼</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 My uncle is such a bore that guests leave early.  我叔叔特别无趣，客人们都早早离席。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 disappoint</td><td>/ˌdɪsəˈpɔɪnt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使失望</td><td>disappoint her loyal fans<br>让忠实粉丝失望</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The sequel disappointed almost everyone who loved the first film.  这部续集让几乎所有喜欢第一部的人都失望了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 disgust</td><td>/dɪsˈɡʌst/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>使厌恶；厌恶</td><td>disgust even his allies<br>连他的盟友也感到厌恶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The state of the kitchen disgusted the health inspector.  厨房的状况让卫生检查员十分厌恶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 repel</td><td>/rɪˈpel/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>击退，驱退；使厌恶</td><td>repel the attackers<br>击退进攻者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The greasy smell of the kitchen seemed to repel every customer who entered.  厨房油腻的气味似乎让每个进来的顾客都感到厌恶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 repulse</td><td>/rɪˈpʌls/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使厌恶，排斥</td><td>repulsed by the smell<br>被那气味恶心到</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The sight of the rotting fruit repulsed everyone standing near the crate.  腐烂水果的样子让站在箱子旁的每个人都感到恶心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unprepossessing</td><td>/ˌʌnpripəˈzɛsɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不吸引人的，不起眼的</td><td>an unprepossessing little shop<br>毫不起眼的小店</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The restaurant is unprepossessing outside but excellent inside.  这家餐馆外表不起眼，里面却极好。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>entrap</b></td><td>/ɪnˈtræp/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>诱骗</td><td>entrap an unwary buyer<br>诱骗不留神的买主</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The con artist entrapped an unwary buyer with a forged certificate.  骗子用一张伪造证书诱骗了一位不留神的买主。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ensnare</td><td>/ɪnˈsner/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>诱捕，使落入陷阱</td><td>ensnare a wild rabbit<br>捕住一只野兔</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cheap loans ensnared many families in years of debt.  廉价贷款使许多家庭陷入多年债务。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 ensnarl</del></td><td>/ɪnˈsnɑːrl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使纠缠，使陷入混乱</td><td>ensnarl the traffic downtown<br>使市中心交通陷入混乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The fallen crane ensnarled traffic for the entire morning.  倒下的吊车使整个上午的交通陷入混乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 entoil</del></td><td>/ɛnˈtɔɪl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使陷入网罗，诱捕</td><td>entoil the enemy scouts<br>把敌方侦察兵诱入陷阱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The clever plan entoiled the smugglers near the border.  这个巧妙的计划在边境附近诱捕了走私者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mesh</td><td>/meʃ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>诱捕</td><td>mesh the prey<br>捕获猎物</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Dolphins are sometimes meshed in nets meant for other fish.  海豚有时会被捕捉其他鱼类的渔网缠住。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 net</td><td>/ˈnet/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>用网捕捉，抓住</td><td>net a butterfly<br>网住一只蝴蝶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The police netted the thief before he could reach the border.  警察在小偷逃到边境前将他抓获。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 snare</td><td>/sner/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>陷阱，罗网；用陷阱捕捉</td><td>snare a rabbit<br>用套索捕兔</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The hunter set a snare near the stream to catch rabbits.  猎人在溪边设下套索捕兔。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 catch up</td><td>/kætʃ ˈʌp/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>缠住，卷入；赶上</td><td>get caught up in a scandal<br>被卷入一场丑闻</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Several honest clerks were caught up in the fraud investigation.  几名诚实的职员被卷入了这起诈骗案调查。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 disentangle</td><td>/ˌdɪsɪnˈtæŋɡəl/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>解开，理清，使脱离</td><td>disentangle the wires<br>把电线理清</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It took hours to disentangle fact from rumor in the report.  在这份报告中把事实与谣言分开花了好几个小时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 untangle</td><td>/ʌnˈtæŋɡəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>解开纠结，理清</td><td>untangle the knot<br>解开这个结</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The auditors are still trying to untangle the company's finances.  审计人员仍在努力理清这家公司的财务。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>entreat</b></td><td>/ɪnˈtriːt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>恳求</td><td>entreat her to return<br>恳求她回来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She entreated him to return before their mother learned of the quarrel.  她恳求他在母亲得知这场争吵之前回来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 beseech</td><td>/bɪˈsiːtʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>恳求，哀求</td><td>beseech him for mercy<br>恳求他宽恕</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The prisoners beseeched the governor for mercy in a long letter.  囚犯们在一封长信中恳求州长开恩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 besiege</td><td>/bɪˈsiːdʒ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>围攻；不断烦扰</td><td>besiege the city<br>围攻城市</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Reporters besieged the minister with questions about the scandal.  记者们就丑闻不断向部长发问。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 conjure</td><td>/kənˈdʒʊr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>恳求，央求；使浮现于脑海</td><td>conjure him to stay<br>恳求他留下</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She conjured him to stay until the doctor finally arrived.  她恳求他留下，直到医生终于赶来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 implore</td><td>/ˌɪmˈplɔːr/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>恳求，哀求</td><td>implore for help<br>苦苦求助</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She implored the judge to give her brother another chance.  她恳求法官再给她弟弟一次机会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 importune</del></td><td>/ˌɪmpɔːrˈtuːn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>恳求，迫切请求</td><td>importune him for money<br>向他苦苦求钱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Beggars importuned travelers for coins outside the station all evening.  乞丐整晚在车站外向旅客苦苦讨要零钱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 solicit</td><td>/səˈlɪsɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>请求，征求；招揽</td><td>solicit donations<br>募集捐款</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The charity solicited funds from local businesses all winter.  该慈善机构整个冬天向本地企业募捐。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 supplicate</td><td>/ˈsʌplɪkeɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>恳求，乞求</td><td>supplicate for forgiveness<br>乞求宽恕</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The prisoner knelt to supplicate for forgiveness before the military tribunal.  囚犯跪下，在军事法庭前乞求宽恕。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 plead to</td><td>/pliːd tuː/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>向…恳求，请求</td><td>plead to the judge<br>向法官恳求</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The prisoner pleaded to the judge for a lighter sentence.  囚犯向法官恳求减轻判刑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 appeal to</td><td>/əˈpiːl tuː/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>向…呼吁，恳求</td><td>appeal to the public<br>向公众呼吁</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The charity appealed to donors for urgent help after the flood.  洪灾后慈善机构向捐赠者紧急求助。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>enunciate</b></td><td>/ɪˈnʌnsieɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>动词、及物动词</td><td>清晰地说；公开宣布，宣称</td><td>enunciate each word clearly<br>清晰吐出每个词</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Please enunciate each word clearly so the people at the back can hear.  请把每个词清晰地吐出，好让后排的人听清。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 articulate</td><td>/ɑːrˈtɪkjuleɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>动词、形容词</td><td>清晰地表达；表达清晰的</td><td>articulate her concerns<br>清晰表达她的顾虑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She managed to articulate her concerns clearly at the board meeting.  她在董事会上清晰地表达了自己的顾虑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 annunciate</del></td><td>/əˈnʌnsieɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>宣布，宣告</td><td>annunciate the verdict<br>宣布判决结果</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Trumpets annunciated the arrival of the queen at the palace gate.  号角宣告女王已抵达宫门。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 broadcast</td><td>/ˈbrɔːdˌkæst/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>广播，播送；散布</td><td>broadcast the news<br>播报消息</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The station broadcast the news of the rescue every hour.  电台每小时播报一次营救消息。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 declare</td><td>/dɪˈkler/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>宣布，声明，断言</td><td>declare one's intentions<br>表明自己的意图</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge declared the earlier verdict invalid and ordered a retrial.  法官宣布先前判决无效并下令重审。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 herald</td><td>/ˈherəld/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>预示，预兆</td><td>herald a new era<br>预示新时代来临</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The invention heralded a new era in communication.  这项发明预示着通信新时代的到来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 proclaim</td><td>/prəˈkleɪm/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>宣告，使…公之于众</td><td>proclaim independence<br>宣布独立</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The colony voted to proclaim independence from the empire that summer.  那年夏天，殖民地投票宣告脱离帝国独立。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 promulgate</td><td>/ˈprɑːməlɡeɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>正式宣布</td><td>promulgate a new law<br>颁布新法律</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The government will promulgate a new law on data privacy next month.  政府将于下月正式颁布一项新的数据隐私法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 publicize</td><td>/ˈpʌblɪsaɪz/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>宣传，公布</td><td>publicize the event<br>宣传这场活动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Organizers publicized the concert with posters across the whole campus.  组织者用海报在全校宣传这场音乐会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 release</td><td>/riˈliːs/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>释放，解除束缚；发布</td><td>release the prisoners<br>释放囚犯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The court ordered officials to release him from custody at once.  法庭命令官员立即将他从拘留中释放。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 give out</td><td>/ˌɡɪv ˈaʊt/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>发出，散发；分发</td><td>give out heat<br>散发热量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old radiator still gives out a surprising amount of heat.  这台旧暖气片散发的热量仍出人意料地多。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>ephemeral</b></td><td>/ɪˈfemərəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>短暂的，转瞬即逝的</td><td>ephemeral fame<br>短暂的名声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Internet fame is often ephemeral and quickly forgotten.  网络名声往往短暂，很快被遗忘。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 evanescent</td><td>/ˌevəˈnesnt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>短暂的，逐渐消失的</td><td>evanescent beauty<br>转瞬即逝的美</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The evanescent glow of fireflies lit the summer path.  萤火虫短暂的微光照亮了夏夜小径。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fleeting</td><td>/ˈfliːtɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>短暂的；转瞬即逝的</td><td>a fleeting moment<br>短暂的瞬间</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 For one fleeting moment the clouds parted above the valley.  有一个短暂的瞬间，云在山谷上方散开了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 impermanent</td><td>/ɪmˈpɜːrmənənt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>非永久的，短暂的</td><td>impermanent beauty<br>短暂的美</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 All fashions are impermanent, replaced by new styles within a few seasons.  一切时尚都是短暂的，几季之内就被新风格取代。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 temporary</td><td>/ˈtempəreri/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>暂时的，临时的</td><td>a temporary solution<br>临时的解决办法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company hired temporary staff to handle the busy holiday season.  公司雇了临时员工来应对繁忙的假日季。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 transient</td><td>/ˈtrænʃənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>短暂的；暂时的；过往的</td><td>transient population<br>流动人口</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Fame in the internet age is often transient and quickly forgotten.  网络时代的名声往往短暂，很快被人遗忘。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 transitory</td><td>/ˈtrænsətɔːri/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>短暂的</td><td>transitory pleasure<br>短暂的快乐</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His anger was transitory and he apologized within the hour.  他的愤怒很短暂，一小时内就道了歉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 fugacious</del></td><td>/fjuːˈɡeɪʃəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>短暂的，易消失的</td><td>fugacious blossoms<br>转瞬即逝的花朵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The fugacious scent of the blossoms vanished after a single warm afternoon.  花朵转瞬即逝的香气在一个暖午之后消失了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fugitive</td><td>/ˈfjuːdʒətɪv/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>短暂的，易逝的；逃亡的</td><td>a fugitive impression<br>转瞬即逝的印象</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A fugitive smile crossed her face before she resumed her serious expression.  一丝转瞬即逝的笑意掠过她的脸。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 eternal</td><td>/ɪˈtɜːrnl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>永恒的</td><td>eternal life<br>永生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The poem promises the beloved eternal youth.  这首诗许诺爱人永恒的青春。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 permanent</td><td>/ˈpɜːrmənənt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>永恒的</td><td>a permanent solution<br>永久的解决办法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They are seeking a permanent solution rather than another quick fix.  他们在寻找永久的解决办法，而不是又一个权宜之计。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 perpetual</td><td>/pərˈpetʃuəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>永恒的，不断的</td><td>perpetual motion<br>永恒运动，永动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He lives in perpetual fear of losing his job.  他一直生活在失业的恐惧中。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 enduring</td><td>/ɪnˈdʊrɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>持久的，长存的</td><td>an enduring friendship<br>长久的友谊</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their enduring friendship survived decades of separation.  他们持久的友谊经历了数十年的分离。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 everlasting</td><td>/ˌevərˈlæstɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>永恒的，持久的</td><td>everlasting peace<br>永久的和平</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They vowed everlasting loyalty to each other.  他们发誓永远彼此忠诚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ceaseless</td><td>/ˈsiːslɪs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不停的，持续的</td><td>ceaseless noise<br>不停的噪音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The ceaseless traffic outside made it impossible for us to sleep.  外面不停的车流让我们无法入睡。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 immortal</td><td>/ɪˈmɔːrtəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>不朽的，永生的</td><td>immortal fame<br>不朽的声名</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The poet's immortal lines are still quoted two thousand years later.  这位诗人不朽的诗句两千年后仍被引用。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 undying</td><td>/ʌnˈdaɪɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>永恒的，不灭的</td><td>undying love<br>永恒的爱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She pledged her undying loyalty to the cause she had joined.  她宣誓对所投身的事业永葆忠诚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>epic</b></td><td>/ˈepɪk/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>宏大的，超凡脱俗的</td><td>an epic journey<br>宏大的旅程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their epic journey across the desert took nearly four months on foot.  他们徒步穿越沙漠的宏大旅程花了近四个月。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 august</td><td>/ɔːˈɡʌst/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>威严的，庄重的</td><td>an august assembly<br>庄严的集会</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The august assembly of scholars rose when the old president entered.  当老校长进场时，这个威严的学者集会全体起立。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 grandiose</td><td>/ˈɡrændioʊs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>自命不凡的，浮夸的</td><td>grandiose plans<br>浮夸的计划</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His grandiose plans for the company never survived contact with reality.  他为公司制定的浮夸计划一遇现实便破灭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 imposing</td><td>/ɪmˈpoʊzɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>宏伟壮丽的</td><td>an imposing building<br>宏伟的建筑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An imposing stone building dominates the square at the city center.  一座气势宏伟的石砌建筑主宰着市中心的广场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 magnificent</td><td>/mæɡˈnɪfɪsnt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>壮丽的</td><td>a magnificent view<br>壮丽的景色</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 From the summit we enjoyed a magnificent view of the whole valley.  从山顶我们欣赏到整个山谷的壮丽景色。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 majestic</td><td>/məˈdʒestɪk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>宏伟壮丽的</td><td>majestic mountains<br>雄伟的群山</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The majestic peaks rose above the clouds in the morning light.  雄伟的山峰在晨光中耸立于云端。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 monumental</td><td>/ˌmɑːnjəˈmentəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>意义重大的；巨大的</td><td>a monumental achievement<br>意义重大的成就</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Building the cathedral was a monumental task that took two centuries.  建造这座大教堂是耗时两个世纪的浩大工程。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 humble</td><td>/ˈhʌmbl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>卑微的，地位低下的；谦逊的</td><td>humble origins<br>卑微的出身</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He rose from humble origins to lead the company.  他出身卑微，最终领导了这家公司。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 unheroic</del></td><td>/ˌʌnhɪˈroʊɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>非英勇的，平庸的</td><td>an unheroic retreat<br>不光彩的撤退</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His unheroic decision to flee disappointed everyone who had trusted him.  他不英勇的逃跑决定让所有信任他的人失望。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unimpressive</td><td>/ˌʌnɪmˈpresɪv/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>平淡无奇的，不起眼的</td><td>an unimpressive debut<br>平淡的首次登场</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His unimpressive debut gave no hint of the fame ahead.  他平淡的首次登场丝毫看不出日后的名声。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 modest</td><td>/ˈmɑːdəst/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>谦虚的；不大的，适度的</td><td>modest about her success<br>对成功很谦虚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He stayed modest about his award and thanked his team.  他对自己的奖项很谦虚，还感谢了团队。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>epicure</b></td><td>/ˈepɪkjʊr/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>美食家</td><td>a noted epicure<br>知名的美食家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A noted epicure, he could name the region of any cheese he tasted.  他是知名的美食家，任何奶酪一尝就能说出产区。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bon vivant</td><td>/ˌbɑn viˈvɑnt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>讲究吃喝、会享受生活的人</td><td>a famous bon vivant<br>有名的享乐家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 As a bon vivant, he knew every good restaurant in Paris.  作为一个懂享受的人，他熟知巴黎每家好餐厅。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 gastronomist</del></td><td>/ɡæˈstrɑnəmɪst/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>美食家，烹饪学家</td><td>a serious gastronomist<br>认真的美食家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The gastronomist wrote a column comparing regional dumplings across the country.  这位美食家写专栏比较全国各地的饺子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gourmand</td><td>/ˈɡʊrmɑnd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>贪吃的人；美食家</td><td>a hearty gourmand<br>大快朵颐的食客</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The gourmand finished three courses before the rest of us started.  这位贪吃者在我们开始前就吃完了三道菜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gourmet</td><td>/ɡʊrˈmeɪ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>美食家</td><td>a gourmet restaurant<br>高级美食餐厅</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 As a serious gourmet, he traveled across three provinces for one soup.  作为一位认真的美食家，他为一道汤跨越了三个省。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 2

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>epigram</b></td><td>/ˈepɪɡræm/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>机智的短诗，警句</td><td>a witty epigram<br>一句机智的警句</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A witty epigram at the end of the essay made everyone smile.  文章末尾一句机智的警句让所有人都笑了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 adage</td><td>/ˈædədʒ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>格言，谚语</td><td>an old adage<br>一句老话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old adage says that haste often makes waste.  老话说，欲速则不达。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 aphorism</td><td>/ˈæfərɪzəm/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>格言，警句</td><td>a well-known aphorism<br>一句有名的警句</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She ended every letter with the same well-worn aphorism about patience.  她每封信都以同一句关于耐心的老套格言结尾。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 apothegm</del></td><td>/ˈæpəθɛm/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>格言，箴言</td><td>a famous apothegm<br>著名的格言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He ended every lecture with an apothegm his grandfather had taught him.  他每次讲座都以祖父教他的一句格言结尾。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 byword</td><td>/ˈbaɪˌwɜːrd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>代名词，典型例子；谚语</td><td>a byword for luxury<br>奢华的代名词</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The brand became a byword for reliability among long-distance drivers.  这个品牌成了长途司机眼中可靠的代名词。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 maxim</td><td>/ˈmæksəm/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>格言，准则</td><td>follow a simple maxim<br>遵循一条简单准则</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her favorite maxim was that practice always beats talent.  她最喜欢的格言是勤奋胜于天赋。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 proverb</td><td>/ˈprɑːvərb/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>谚语，格言</td><td>an ancient Chinese proverb<br>一句中国古谚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A Chinese proverb warns against judging people by appearance.  一句中国谚语告诫不要以外貌评判他人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>epilogue</b></td><td>/ˈepɪlɔːɡ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>文学作品的结局</td><td>a brief epilogue<br>简短的结语</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A brief epilogue tells the reader what became of the two brothers.  简短的结语告诉读者两兄弟后来的境况。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 preface</td><td>/ˈprefəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>序言</td><td>write a preface<br>写序言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The author used the preface to thank everyone who helped her research.  作者在序言中感谢了所有帮助她做研究的人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>epithet</b></td><td>/ˈepɪθet/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>外号，绰号</td><td>an insulting epithet<br>侮辱性的称号</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He shouted an insulting epithet at the referee and was sent off.  他冲裁判喊出一个侮辱性的称号，结果被罚下场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 alias</td><td>/ˈeɪliəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>别名，化名</td><td>travel under an alias<br>用化名旅行</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The writer published her early novels under an alias to avoid attention.  这位作家用化名出版早期小说以避免关注。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cognomen</td><td>/kɑɡˈnoʊmən/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>姓；绰号，别名</td><td>an affectionate cognomen<br>亲昵的绰号</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His teammates gave him a cognomen that stuck for twenty years.  队友给他起的绰号沿用了二十年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sobriquet</td><td>/ˈsoʊbrəˌkeɪ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>绰号，诨名</td><td>earn a sobriquet<br>得到一个绰号</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The city earned the sobriquet of the Windy City long ago.  这座城市很早就得了风城的绰号。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>epitomize</b></td><td>/ɪˈpɪtəmaɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>代表，体现，是…的典型范例</td><td>epitomize the era<br>是那个时代的典型</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 These narrow streets epitomize the era before automobiles reshaped the whole city.  这些狭窄的街道正是汽车重塑整座城市之前那个时代的典型。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 body</td><td>/ˈbɑːdi/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>体现，具体表现；身体</td><td>body forth an idea<br>体现某种思想</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The statue seems to body forth every ideal the founders cherished.  这座雕像仿佛体现了创建者珍视的每一个理想。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 express</td><td>/ɪkˈspres/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、形容词</td><td>表达，表现；快速的</td><td>express deep gratitude<br>表达深切感谢</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She struggled to express the gratitude she felt toward her mentor.  她难以表达对导师的感激之情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 externalize</td><td>/ɪkˈstɜːrnəlaɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使外在化，使具体表现</td><td>externalize inner conflict<br>把内心冲突外化</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Painting helped him externalize feelings he could never put into words.  绘画帮他把无法言说的情感外化出来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 incarnate</td><td>/ɪnˈkɑːrneɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词、形容词</td><td>使（思想、理论）具化，体现</td><td>incarnate an ideal<br>体现一种理想</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Many people think Hitler incarnated pure evil and boundless self-regard.  许多人认为希特勒是纯粹邪恶与极端自我的化身。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 incorporate</td><td>/ɪnˈkɔːrpəreɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、形容词</td><td>包含，纳入；体现</td><td>incorporate new ideas<br>纳入新想法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The revised design incorporates suggestions from every member of the team.  修订后的设计纳入了团队每位成员的建议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 instantiate</td><td>/ɪnˈstænʃieɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>举例说明；使具体化</td><td>instantiate a theory<br>例证一种理论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 These fossils instantiate the pattern the biologist predicted decades ago.  这些化石印证了生物学家几十年前预言的模式。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 manifest</td><td>/ˈmænɪfest/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>显然的，明显易懂的；显现，显露</td><td>manifest injustice<br>明显的不公</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His discomfort at the question was manifest to everyone seated around the table.  他对这个问题的不适让围坐桌边的每个人都看得明明白白。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 materialize</td><td>/məˈtɪriəˌlaɪz/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>实现，成为现实；具体化</td><td>fail to materialize<br>未能实现</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The promised funding never materialized, so the project was quietly dropped.  承诺的资金始终没到位，项目被悄悄放弃。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 substantiate</td><td>/səbˈstænʃieɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>证实</td><td>substantiate the claim<br>证实这一说法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 No evidence has emerged to substantiate his serious accusations.  没有证据能证实他严重的指控。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>equable</b></td><td>/ˈekwəbl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>（脾气、性情）温和的</td><td>an equable temper<br>平和的性情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His equable nature made him easy to work with.  他性情平和，与他共事很轻松。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 balmy</td><td>/ˈbɑːmi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>温和宜人的</td><td>a balmy evening<br>温暖宜人的傍晚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We sat outside enjoying the balmy air of early summer.  我们坐在外面享受初夏温和宜人的空气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 genial</td><td>/ˈdʒiːniəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>和蔼亲切的；温和宜人的</td><td>a genial host<br>亲切的主人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Our genial neighbor invited the whole street over for dinner.  我们和蔼可亲的邻居请整条街的人来吃晚饭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gentle</td><td>/ˈdʒentəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>温和的，轻柔的</td><td>a gentle voice<br>温柔的声音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He gave the door a gentle push and stepped inside.  他轻轻推了一下门，走了进去。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 moderate</td><td>/ˈmɑːdəreɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词、形容词</td><td>缓和，减轻；适度的</td><td>moderate one's demands<br>降低自己的要求</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He agreed to moderate his tone during the debate.  他同意在辩论中缓和语气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 temperate</td><td>/ˈtempərət/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>（言行举止）有分寸的，有节制的</td><td>a temperate response<br>有分寸的回应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave a temperate reply to a very rude question.  面对无礼的问题她的回答很有分寸。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 harsh</td><td>/hɑːrʃ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>严厉的</td><td>harsh criticism<br>严厉的批评</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge's harsh sentence surprised even the prosecutors in the courtroom.  法官严厉的判决连法庭上的检察官都感到意外。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 inclement</td><td>/ɪnˈklemənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>（天气等）恶劣的</td><td>inclement weather<br>恶劣的天气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The ceremony moved indoors because of unusually inclement weather.  由于天气异常恶劣，典礼改在室内举行。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 intemperate</td><td>/ɪnˈtempərət/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无节制的，极端的，不温和的</td><td>intemperate language<br>过激的言辞</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His intemperate language during the debate cost him the support of moderates.  他在辩论中过激的言辞使他失去了温和派的支持。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 severe</td><td>/səˈvɪr/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>严厉的；严重的</td><td>a severe penalty<br>严厉的处罚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge imposed a severe penalty on the repeat offender.  法官对累犯施以严厉处罚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 agitation</td><td>/ˌædʒəˈteɪʃən/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>焦虑不安，激动</td><td>in a state of agitation<br>处于焦躁不安状态</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He paced the hallway in a state of visible agitation.  他在走廊里踱步，显得焦躁不安。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 excitability</td><td>/ɪkˌsaɪtəˈbɪləti/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>易兴奋，易激动</td><td>nervous excitability<br>神经易兴奋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His excitability made him a poor choice for delicate negotiations.  他易激动的性格使他不适合微妙的谈判。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>equity</b></td><td>/ˈekwəti/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>不偏不倚，公平</td><td>equity in education<br>教育公平</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The reform aims at greater equity in education between rural and urban schools.  这项改革旨在促进城乡学校之间更大的教育公平。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 disinterestedness</del></td><td>/dɪsˈɪntrəstədnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>不感兴趣；无私公正</td><td>an air of disinterestedness<br>一副漠不关心的样子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His apparent disinterestedness hid a very strong personal ambition.  他表面的漠不关心掩藏着极强的个人野心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 evenhandedness</del></td><td>/ˌiːvənˈhændɪdnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>公平，不偏不倚</td><td>judicial evenhandedness<br>司法公正</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The referee earned respect for his evenhandedness in a heated match.  裁判在激烈比赛中的公正赢得了尊重。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fairness</td><td>/ˈfernəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>公平，公正</td><td>a sense of fairness<br>公平感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Students accepted the grading system because of its obvious fairness.  学生们因评分制度明显公平而接受了它。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 impartiality</td><td>/ˌɪmpɑːrʃiˈæləti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>公正，不偏袒</td><td>judge with impartiality<br>公正地裁断</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A juror must maintain impartiality despite pressure from public opinion.  陪审员必须在舆论压力下保持公正。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 neutrality</td><td>/nuːˈtræləti/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>中立，不偏不倚</td><td>maintain strict neutrality<br>保持严格中立</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The small country preserved its neutrality throughout both world wars.  这个小国在两次世界大战中都保持中立。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 nonpartisanship</del></td><td>/ˌnɑnˈpɑrtəzənʃɪp/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>无党派性，不偏袒</td><td>commitment to nonpartisanship<br>坚持不偏不倚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The commission defended its nonpartisanship when both parties attacked its report.  两党都攻击报告时，委员会为自身的不偏不倚辩护。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 bias</td><td>/ˈbaɪəs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>偏见，偏袒</td><td>free from bias<br>没有偏见</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Reviewers were kept anonymous to reduce bias in judging the papers.  评审匿名以减少论文评判中的偏见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 favoritism</td><td>/ˈfeɪvərɪtɪzəm/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>偏爱，偏袒</td><td>show blatant favoritism<br>明显偏袒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Promoting his own nephew looked like blatant favoritism to everyone.  提拔自己的外甥在所有人看来都是明显的偏袒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 nonobjectivity</del></td><td>/ˌnɑnɑbdʒɛkˈtɪvəti/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>非客观性，主观偏见</td><td>criticized for nonobjectivity<br>因不客观受批评</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report's nonobjectivity was obvious to anyone who checked the sources.  任何核对来源的人都能看出报告不客观。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 one-sidedness</td><td>/ˌwʌnˈsaɪdɪdnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>片面性，偏向一方</td><td>the one-sidedness of coverage<br>报道的片面性</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Readers complained about the one-sidedness of the newspaper's election coverage.  读者抱怨报纸选举报道的片面性。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 partiality</td><td>/ˌpɑːrʃiˈæləti/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>偏爱；偏袒</td><td>a partiality for sweets<br>对甜食的偏爱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The coach was accused of partiality toward his own students.  教练被指责偏袒自己的学生。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 partisanship</td><td>/ˈpɑːrtəzənˌʃɪp/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>党派偏见，偏袒</td><td>bitter partisanship<br>激烈的党派之争</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Partisanship blocked every attempt to pass the compromise budget bill.  党派偏见阻碍了通过折中预算案的一切努力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 prejudice</td><td>/ˈpredʒədɪs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>偏见，成见</td><td>racial prejudice<br>种族偏见</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years of contact slowly wore away his prejudice against the newcomers.  多年接触慢慢消磨了他对新来者的偏见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 biased</td><td>/ˈbaɪəst/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有偏见的，偏向的</td><td>a biased report<br>有偏见的报道</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Readers complained that the coverage was clearly biased.  读者抱怨这些报道明显带有偏见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 discriminatory</td><td>/dɪˈskrɪmɪnətɔːri/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>歧视性的，不公平的</td><td>discriminatory hiring practices<br>歧视性的招聘做法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The court struck down the discriminatory rule as plainly unconstitutional.  法院以明显违宪为由推翻了该歧视性规定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>equivalent</b></td><td>/ɪˈkwɪvələnt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>等价的，相等的</td><td>the equivalent amount<br>等量的数额</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They offered her the equivalent amount in shares instead of cash.  他们提出用等量的股份代替现金付给她。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 coequal</td><td>/ˌkoʊˈiːkwəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>同等的，地位平等的</td><td>coequal branches of government<br>地位平等的政府分支</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The constitution treats the three branches as coequal partners in governing.  宪法把三个分支视为地位平等的治理伙伴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 coordinate</td><td>/koʊˈɔːrdɪneɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词、形容词</td><td>协调，使配合</td><td>coordinate the relief effort<br>协调救援工作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two agencies coordinated the rescue effort after the flood.  洪水过后两个机构协调了救援工作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 counterpart</td><td>/ˈkaʊntərpɑːrt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>（地位、功能）对等的人或物</td><td>his French counterpart<br>他的法国对等人物</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Our finance minister met his German counterpart to discuss the trade dispute.  我们的财政部长会见德国的对等官员，商讨贸易争端。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 peer</td><td>/pɪr/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、不及物动词</td><td>同等地位的人，同辈；好奇地凝视</td><td>respected by his peers<br>受同辈尊重</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His work is widely respected by his peers in the field.  他的研究在业内同辈中广受尊重。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rival</td><td>/ˈraɪvəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>对手，竞争者</td><td>a bitter rival<br>势不两立的对手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two firms have been rivals in this market for decades.  这两家公司在该市场竞争了数十年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 parallel</td><td>/ˈperəˌlel/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>相似的，对应的；平行的</td><td>a parallel case<br>类似的案例</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Historians found a parallel case in a much earlier war.  历史学家在一场更早的战争中找到了类似的例子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>equivocate</b></td><td>/ɪˈkwɪvəkeɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>（带有欺骗目的地）模棱两可地说，说谎话</td><td>equivocate under questioning<br>受问时含糊其辞</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She equivocated under questioning about the true state of her finances.  在被问及财务真实状况时，她含糊其辞。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fudge</td><td>/ˈfʌdʒ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>含糊其词，回避；软糖</td><td>fudge the issue<br>回避问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The spokesman fudged the question about layoffs for twenty painful minutes.  发言人就裁员问题含糊其词了痛苦的二十分钟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hedge</td><td>/hedʒ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>树篱，篱笆；用篱笆围住</td><td>a neatly trimmed hedge<br>修剪整齐的树篱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A neatly trimmed hedge separated the garden from the road.  一道修剪整齐的树篱把花园与马路隔开。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 weasel</td><td>/ˈwiːzəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>耍滑逃避（责任）；黄鼠狼</td><td>weasel out of a promise<br>耍滑推掉承诺</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He tried to weasel out of paying his share.  他试图耍滑不付自己那份钱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 prevaricate</td><td>/prɪˈværɪkeɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>支吾其词，撒谎</td><td>prevaricate about the facts<br>就事实支吾其词</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Witnesses began to prevaricate whenever the lawyer asked about the missing money.  每当律师问到失踪的钱，证人就开始支吾其词。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 palter</del></td><td>/ˈpɔːltər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>欺骗；讨价还价</td><td>palter with the truth<br>对真相含糊其词</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Witnesses should not palter with the truth when giving sworn testimony.  证人在宣誓作证时不应对真相含糊其词。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 communicate straightforwardly</td><td>/kəˈmjuːnɪkeɪt ˌstreɪtˈfɔrwərdli/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>坦率明确地表达</td><td>communicate straightforwardly with staff<br>与员工坦率沟通</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Good managers communicate straightforwardly instead of hiding bad news in jargon.  好的管理者坦率沟通，而不是用行话掩盖坏消息。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 clarity</td><td>/ˈklærəti/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>清晰，清楚</td><td>with great clarity<br>非常清晰地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She explained the complicated rules with surprising clarity.  她把复杂的规则解释得异常清晰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>empirical</b></td><td>/ɪmˈpɪrɪkl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>基于观察、实验的</td><td>empirical evidence<br>实证证据</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The theory sounds elegant but lacks empirical evidence from actual field trials.  这个理论听着优雅，却缺少来自实地试验的实证证据。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 existential</td><td>/ˌeɡzɪˈstenʃəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>存在的，与存在有关的</td><td>an existential threat<br>生存性威胁</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Climate change poses an existential threat to low-lying island nations.  气候变化对低地岛国构成生存威胁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 experiential</td><td>/ɪkˌspɪriˈenʃəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>基于经验的，体验的</td><td>experiential learning<br>体验式学习</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The course emphasizes experiential learning through fieldwork rather than lectures.  这门课强调通过田野作业进行体验式学习。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 experimental</td><td>/ɪkˌsperɪˈmentəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>实验的，试验性的</td><td>experimental data<br>实验数据</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The drug remains experimental and is not yet approved for sale.  该药仍处试验阶段，尚未获批销售。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 objective</td><td>/əbˈdʒektɪv/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>客观的，公正的；目标</td><td>an objective assessment<br>客观的评估</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Reporters are expected to remain objective about the candidates.  记者应对候选人保持客观。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 observational</td><td>/ˌɑːbzərˈveɪʃənəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>观察的，基于观察的</td><td>observational studies<br>观察性研究</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Observational studies suggest a link that trials have not yet confirmed.  观察性研究提示一种试验尚未证实的关联。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 nonempirical</del></td><td>/ˌnɑnɛmˈpɪrɪkəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>非实证的，非经验的</td><td>nonempirical reasoning<br>非实证的推理</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Purely nonempirical arguments rarely persuade scientists who demand hard data.  纯粹非实证的论证很难说服要求硬数据的科学家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 theoretical</td><td>/ˌθiːəˈretɪkəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>理论的，理论上的</td><td>purely theoretical physics<br>纯理论物理</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His objection is theoretical and has no bearing on daily practice.  他的反对是理论性的，与日常实践无关。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 unempirical</del></td><td>/ˌʌnɛmˈpɪrɪkəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>非经验的，缺乏实证的</td><td>an unempirical claim<br>缺乏实证的主张</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Editors rejected the paper as unempirical because it cited no measurements.  编辑因论文未引用任何测量数据而退稿。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>errant</b></td><td>/ˈerənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>误入歧途的，犯错误的</td><td>an errant husband<br>出轨的丈夫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The errant student was suspended after ignoring three separate warnings.  这名行为不端的学生在忽视三次警告后被停学。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 misbehaving</td><td>/ˌmɪsbɪˈheɪvɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>行为不端的，捣乱的</td><td>misbehaving students<br>捣乱的学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The teacher quietly separated the two misbehaving boys.  老师悄悄把那两个捣乱的男孩分开。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mischievous</td><td>/ˈmɪstʃɪvəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有害的；调皮的</td><td>a mischievous rumor<br>有害的谣言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mischievous rumor damaged her reputation for several years.  这个有害的谣言损害了她多年的名誉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 behaved</td><td>/bɪˈheɪvd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>举止规矩的，守规矩的</td><td>a well behaved child<br>守规矩的孩子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their dog is remarkably well behaved even around excited small children.  他们的狗即使在兴奋的小孩身边也非常守规矩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 behaving</td><td>/bɪˈheɪvɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>举止得体的，守规矩的</td><td>well-behaving students<br>守规矩的学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Teachers rewarded the well-behaving students with an extra afternoon outdoors.  老师奖励守规矩的学生多一个下午的户外时间。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 nice</td><td>/ˈnaɪs/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>规矩得体的；令人愉快的</td><td>nice manners<br>得体的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He is a nice boy who never gives his parents trouble.  他是个规矩的男孩，从不给父母添麻烦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 orderly</td><td>/ˈɔːrdərli/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有秩序的，整齐的</td><td>an orderly queue<br>整齐的队伍</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The students left the hall in an orderly line.  学生们排成整齐的队伍离开礼堂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 3

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>erratic</b></td><td>/ɪˈrætɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>反复无常的，不规律的</td><td>erratic driving behavior<br>飘忽不定的驾驶行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His erratic driving behavior alarmed the passengers long before the police stopped him.  早在警察拦下他之前，他飘忽不定的驾驶行为就让乘客惊慌。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 changing</td><td>/ˈtʃeɪndʒɪŋ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>变化的，不断改变的</td><td>changing weather<br>变化的天气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Farmers must adapt to changing rainfall patterns across the growing season.  农民必须适应生长季中变化的降雨模式。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fluctuating</td><td>/ˈflʌktʃuˌeɪtɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>波动的，起伏不定的</td><td>fluctuating prices<br>波动的价格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Fluctuating oil prices made it hard to plan next year's budget.  波动的油价让明年的预算难以制定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 irregular</td><td>/ɪˈreɡjələr/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不规则的，不平整的</td><td>an irregular shape<br>不规则的形状</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The path was made of irregular stones of many sizes.  这条小路由各种大小不规则的石头铺成。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unequal</td><td>/ʌnˈiːkwəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不相等的，不平等的</td><td>unequal treatment<br>不平等的待遇</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two sides entered the contest on decidedly unequal terms.  双方是在明显不平等的条件下参赛的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unstable</td><td>/ʌnˈsteɪbəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不稳定的，易变的</td><td>an unstable currency<br>不稳定的货币</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The ladder felt unstable, so we refused to climb any higher.  梯子感觉不稳，我们不肯再往上爬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unsteady</td><td>/ʌnˈstedi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不稳的，摇晃的</td><td>unsteady hands<br>不稳的手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her voice was unsteady as she read the letter to us.  她给我们读那封信时声音颤抖不稳。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 varying</td><td>/ˈveriɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不同的，变化的</td><td>varying degrees of success<br>不同程度的成功</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The students finished the task with varying degrees of accuracy.  学生完成任务的准确度各不相同。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 changeless</td><td>/ˈtʃeɪndʒləs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不变的，永恒的</td><td>a changeless landscape<br>恒久不变的景色</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The village seemed changeless, though the world outside had transformed completely.  村子好像从未改变，尽管外面的世界已彻底变样。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 constant</td><td>/ˈkɑːnstənt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>不变的，恒定的；忠诚的</td><td>a constant friend<br>忠诚不变的朋友</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her support remained constant through every difficult year.  她的支持在每个艰难年头都不曾改变。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 stable</td><td>/ˈsteɪbəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>稳定的；马厩</td><td>a stable income<br>稳定的收入</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His condition has been stable since the operation last Tuesday.  自上周二手术以来他的病情一直稳定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 steady</td><td>/ˈstedi/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>稳定的，持续不变的</td><td>a steady stream of visitors<br>络绎不绝的来访者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Sales have shown steady growth for eight straight quarters.  销售额已连续八个季度稳定增长。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unchanging</td><td>/ʌnˈtʃeɪndʒɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不变的，始终如一的</td><td>unchanging routine<br>一成不变的作息</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her unchanging kindness comforted everyone who worked in that busy office.  她始终如一的善意安慰了那间忙碌办公室里的每个人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unvarying</td><td>/ʌnˈvɛriɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无变化的，始终一致的</td><td>an unvarying rhythm<br>一成不变的节奏</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The machine repeated the task at an unvarying speed all night.  机器整夜以一成不变的速度重复这项工作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>erudite</b></td><td>/ˈerudaɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>博学的</td><td>an erudite scholar<br>博学的学者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The erudite professor could quote ancient poetry from memory for hours.  那位博学的教授能凭记忆连续几小时引用古诗。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 knowledgeable</td><td>/ˈnɑːlədʒəbəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>知识丰富的，了解很多的</td><td>knowledgeable about wine<br>懂葡萄酒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Our guide was remarkably knowledgeable about local birds.  我们的导游对当地鸟类了解得非常多。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 learned</td><td>/ˈlɜːrnɪd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>博学的，有学问的</td><td>a learned professor<br>博学的教授</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The learned judge quoted three languages in a single short opinion.  这位博学的法官在一份简短意见中引用了三种语言。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lettered</td><td>/ˈletərd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有学问的，受过教育的</td><td>a lettered gentleman<br>有学问的绅士</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only a lettered few could read the inscriptions on those tombs.  只有少数有学问的人能读懂那些墓上的铭文。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 literate</td><td>/ˈlɪtərət/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有读写能力的；有学识的</td><td>a literate population<br>有文化的人口</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Barely half the adults in that region were literate a century ago.  一个世纪前该地区成年人中识字的勉强过半。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scholarly</td><td>/ˈskɑːlərli/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>学术的，博学的</td><td>a scholarly article<br>学术文章</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His scholarly habits kept him in the archive until closing time.  他学者式的习惯让他在档案馆待到闭馆。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 well-read</td><td>/ˌwɛl ˈrɛd/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>博览群书的，博学的</td><td>a well-read editor<br>博览群书的编辑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She is well-read enough to place any quotation within seconds.  她博览群书，能几秒内说出任何引文的出处。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ignorant</td><td>/ˈɪɡnərənt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无知的，不知道的</td><td>ignorant of the facts<br>不了解事实</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They were completely ignorant of the danger ahead.  他们对前方的危险毫不知情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unlettered</td><td>/ˌʌnˈletərd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>未受教育的，文盲的</td><td>unlettered villagers<br>未受教育的村民</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Many unlettered farmers memorized long songs instead of writing them down.  许多未受教育的农民靠记诵长歌，而不是把它们写下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 benighted</td><td>/bɪˈnaɪtɪd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>愚昧无知的；蒙昧落后的</td><td>benighted superstition<br>愚昧的迷信</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He dismissed the whole century as benighted and hopelessly superstitious.  他把整个世纪都视为愚昧且不可救药地迷信。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 illiterate</td><td>/ɪˈlɪtərət/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>文盲的，不识字的</td><td>functionally illiterate adults<br>实际上文盲的成人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Millions remain illiterate because schools in their villages closed years ago.  数百万人仍是文盲，因为他们村里的学校多年前就关了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 uneducated</td><td>/ʌnˈedʒukeɪtɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>未受教育的，无知的</td><td>largely uneducated farm workers<br>大多未受教育的农场工人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Though uneducated, he could repair any engine brought to his shop.  他虽未受过教育，却能修好送到店里的任何发动机。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 unscholarly</del></td><td>/ʌnˈskɑlərli/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>非学术的，不严谨的</td><td>unscholarly guesswork<br>不严谨的猜测</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Reviewers called the footnotes unscholarly and demanded verifiable primary sources.  评审称脚注不够学术，要求提供可核查的原始资料。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>escalate</b></td><td>/ˈeskəleɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>（使）（战争等）升级，扩大</td><td>escalate into violence<br>升级为暴力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The border dispute quickly escalated into a full-scale armed conflict.  边界争端迅速升级为全面武装冲突。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 aggrandize</td><td>/əˈɡrændaɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>扩大（权力、财富、声望）</td><td>aggrandize one's power<br>扩张自己的权力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mayor used the crisis to aggrandize his own power.  市长利用危机扩大自己的权力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 amplify</td><td>/ˈæmpləˌfaɪ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>扩大，增强；详述</td><td>amplify a signal<br>放大信号</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Could you amplify your last remark a little?  你能把最后那句话再详述一下吗？</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 augment</td><td>/ɔɡˈment/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>增加，增强</td><td>augment one's income<br>增加收入</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She augments her salary by teaching evening classes.  她通过教夜校课程增加收入。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 boost</td><td>/ˈbuːst/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>提高，促进；推动</td><td>boost sales<br>促进销售</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The tax cut is expected to boost consumer spending.  减税预计会促进消费者支出。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 expand</td><td>/ɪkˈspænd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>扩大，扩张；详述</td><td>expand the business<br>扩大业务</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company plans to expand into three new markets.  公司计划扩张到三个新市场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pump up</td><td>/pʌmp ˈʌp/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>使增加，抬高；给…充气</td><td>pump up the price<br>抬高价格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Advertising pumped up demand until the small factory could not cope.  广告抬高需求，直到小工厂应付不来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 build up</td><td>/bɪld ˈʌp/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>积聚，逐渐增强</td><td>build up pressure<br>积聚压力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Traffic builds up quickly on this road after five o'clock.  五点以后这条路上的车流迅速积聚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 wane</td><td>/weɪn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>减少，衰退</td><td>wax and wane<br>盈亏消长，兴衰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Public interest in the scandal waned within a month.  公众对这桩丑闻的兴趣一个月内就减退了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 diminish</td><td>/dɪˈmɪnɪʃ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>（使）减少；轻视，贬低</td><td>diminish in importance<br>重要性下降</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His influence diminished steadily after he left the board.  他离开董事会后影响力持续减弱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>eschew</b></td><td>/ɪsˈtʃuː/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>刻意避开；戒绝</td><td>eschew violence<br>戒绝暴力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The monks eschew luxury and live on whatever the village offers.  僧侣们避开奢华，靠村民所给的一切生活。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dodge</td><td>/dɑːdʒ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>躲避</td><td>dodge the question<br>回避问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The minister managed to dodge the question three times in one interview.  部长在一次采访中三次成功回避了这个问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 elude</td><td>/ɪˈluːd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>躲闪，躲避；使无法理解，使困惑</td><td>elude the police<br>躲开警察</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The thief eluded the police for nearly six months before being caught.  小偷躲开警方近六个月才被抓住。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 evade</td><td>/ɪˈveɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>逃避，规避</td><td>evade the question<br>回避问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The witness evaded every question about the missing money.  证人回避了所有关于失踪钱款的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shirk</td><td>/ʃɜːrk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>逃避，规避</td><td>shirk responsibility<br>逃避责任</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 No one on this team is allowed to shirk hard work.  这个团队里没人可以逃避苦活。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shun</td><td>/ʃʌn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>避开，避免</td><td>shun public attention<br>避开公众关注</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The writer chose to shun public attention after winning the prize.  那位作家获奖后选择避开公众关注。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 weasel out of</td><td>/ˈwiːzəl aʊt ʌv/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>推脱，逃避责任</td><td>weasel out of a promise<br>逃避承诺</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He tried to weasel out of the contract by citing vague technicalities.  他试图以含糊的技术细节为由逃避合同。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 embrace</td><td>/ɪmˈbreɪs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>乐于接受；拥抱</td><td>embrace change<br>乐于接受变化</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Young workers quickly embraced the new technology.  年轻员工迅速接受了这项新技术。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 greet</td><td>/ˈɡriːt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>迎接，欢迎；回应</td><td>greet the guests warmly<br>热情迎接客人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She greeted every customer by name as they entered the shop.  客人进店时她都能叫出名字打招呼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 welcome</td><td>/ˈwelkəm/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词、形容词</td><td>欢迎，乐于接受</td><td>welcome the chance to help<br>乐于接受帮忙的机会</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Residents welcomed the decision to reopen the public library.  居民们欢迎重新开放公共图书馆的决定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 habitually indulge in</td><td>/həˈbɪtʃuəli ɪnˈdʌldʒ ɪn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>习惯性沉溺于</td><td>habitually indulge in gossip<br>惯于沉溺闲话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Those who habitually indulge in flattery lose the trust of colleagues.  惯于沉溺于奉承的人会失去同事的信任。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 seek</td><td>/ˈsiːk/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>寻求，追求</td><td>seek professional advice<br>寻求专业建议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Many families seek shelter in the school gym during heavy storms.  暴风雨期间许多家庭到学校体育馆寻求庇护。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>esoteric</b></td><td>/ˌesəˈterɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>深奥难懂的</td><td>esoteric knowledge<br>深奥的知识</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She writes about esoteric topics in medieval music.  她写中世纪音乐中深奥的题目。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 abstruse</td><td>/əbˈstruːs/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>难以理解的</td><td>abstruse theories<br>深奥的理论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His lecture was too abstruse for first-year students.  他的讲座对一年级学生来说太晦涩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 arcane</td><td>/ɑːrˈkeɪn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>深奥的，难以理解的</td><td>arcane rules<br>晦涩难懂的规则</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The tax code is full of arcane rules nobody understands.  税法充满无人理解的晦涩规定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hermetic</td><td>/hərˈmetɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>深奥的；密封的</td><td>a hermetic style<br>晦涩的风格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The poet's hermetic style baffles most readers.  这位诗人晦涩的风格令多数读者困惑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 recondite</td><td>/ˈrekəndaɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>深奥的，难解的</td><td>recondite arguments<br>深奥的论证</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lecture grew so recondite that half the audience quietly left.  讲座变得如此深奥，一半听众悄悄离场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 shallow</td><td>/ˈʃæloʊ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>浅薄的，肤浅的；浅的</td><td>shallow water<br>浅水</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His arguments are shallow and easy to refute.  他的论点浅薄，很容易反驳。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 superficial</td><td>/ˌsuːpərˈfɪʃl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>表面的，肤浅的</td><td>a superficial understanding<br>肤浅的理解</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She has only a superficial knowledge of the subject.  她对这门学科只有肤浅的了解。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>espouse</b></td><td>/ɪˈspaʊz/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>支持，拥护</td><td>espouse a cause<br>拥护一项事业</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She openly espoused the cause of prison reform.  她公开拥护监狱改革事业。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 embrace</td><td>/ɪmˈbreɪs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>乐于接受；拥抱</td><td>embrace change<br>乐于接受变化</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Young workers quickly embraced the new technology.  年轻员工迅速接受了这项新技术。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 take on</td><td>/ˈteɪk ˈɑn/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>雇用；承担</td><td>take on more workers<br>雇用更多工人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The firm plans to take on twenty interns this summer.  公司计划今年夏天招收二十名实习生。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 take up</td><td>/teɪk ˈʌp/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>开始从事；支持，采纳</td><td>take up a cause<br>支持一项事业</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She took up painting after retiring from thirty years of teaching.  她在结束三十年教学生涯后开始学画画。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 abjure</td><td>/əbˈdʒʊr/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>发誓放弃；避免</td><td>abjure violence<br>发誓弃绝暴力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The group publicly abjured violence as a political tool.  该团体公开宣布放弃以暴力作为政治手段。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 repudiate</td><td>/rɪˈpjuːdieɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>否认，拒绝接受</td><td>repudiate a claim<br>否认一项主张</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The author repudiated the views quoted in the article.  作者否认了文章中引用的观点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>esteem</b></td><td>/ɪˈstiːm/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>尊重，敬重</td><td>hold in high esteem<br>十分敬重</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her colleagues esteem her for her honesty and courage.  同事们因她的诚实与勇气而敬重她。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 appreciation</td><td>/əˌpriːʃiˈeɪʃən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>赏识，尊重；感激；增值</td><td>show appreciation for help<br>对帮助表示感激</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He expressed his appreciation for the team that worked all weekend.  他对整个周末工作的团队表示感激。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 estimation</td><td>/ˌestəˈmeɪʃən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>尊重，评价；估计</td><td>rise in my estimation<br>在我心中评价提高</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her honesty raised her greatly in the estimation of her colleagues.  她的诚实大大提升了同事对她的评价。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 favor</td><td>/ˈfeɪvər/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>赞同，好评；恩惠</td><td>win public favor<br>赢得公众好评</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The proposal quickly won favor among the younger members.  这项提议很快赢得年轻成员的支持。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 regard</td><td>/rɪˈɡɑːrd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>关注，重视；看待</td><td>have regard for others<br>顾及他人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He drives fast with little regard for other people.  他开车很快，几乎不顾及他人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 respect</td><td>/rɪˈspekt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>尊敬，敬重；方面</td><td>show respect for<br>对……表示尊敬</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Students should learn to respect opinions that differ from their own.  学生应学会尊重与自己不同的意见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 disfavor</td><td>/dɪsˈfeɪvər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>不赞成；失宠</td><td>fall into disfavor<br>失宠，不受待见</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old method gradually fell into disfavor among researchers.  这种旧方法逐渐不受研究者青睐。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 odium</td><td>/ˈoʊdiəm/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>憎恶，讨厌</td><td>public odium<br>公众的憎恶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Public odium followed the executives long after the company had been dissolved.  公司解散很久之后，公众的憎恶仍伴随着这些高管。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>estimable</b></td><td>/ˈestɪməbl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>值得尊敬的</td><td>an estimable achievement<br>值得称许的成就</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her work in public health is an estimable achievement by any measure.  她在公共卫生领域的工作无论怎么看都是值得尊敬的成就。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 prestigious</td><td>/preˈstɪdʒəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有声望的，受尊敬的</td><td>a prestigious award<br>有声望的奖项</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He turned down a prestigious post to stay near his family.  他为了留在家人身边拒绝了一个有声望的职位。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reputable</td><td>/ˈrepjətəbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>声誉好的，可信的</td><td>a reputable dealer<br>信誉良好的经销商</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Buy the parts from a reputable supplier rather than an auction site.  从信誉良好的供应商买零件，而不是拍卖网站。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 applaudable</del></td><td>/əˈplɔdəbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>值得称赞的</td><td>an applaudable effort<br>值得称赞的努力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her decision to return the money was applaudable and widely reported.  她归还钱款的决定值得称赞，被广泛报道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 commendable</td><td>/kəˈmendəbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>值得赞扬的</td><td>commendable honesty<br>值得赞扬的诚实</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The rescue team showed commendable calm during the long night.  救援队在漫长的夜里表现出值得赞扬的镇定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 creditable</td><td>/ˈkredətəbəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>值得称赞的；相当好的</td><td>a creditable performance<br>值得称赞的表现</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The young violinist gave a creditable account of the concerto.  年轻小提琴手对协奏曲的演绎值得称赞。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 meritorious</td><td>/ˌmerəˈtɔːriəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有功的，值得称赞的</td><td>meritorious service<br>有功的服务</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He received a medal for meritorious conduct under heavy fire.  他因在猛烈炮火下的有功表现获得奖章。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 praiseworthy</td><td>/ˈpreɪzˌwɜːrði/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>值得赞扬的</td><td>a praiseworthy effort<br>值得赞扬的努力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their decision to return the money was entirely praiseworthy.  他们归还钱款的决定完全值得赞扬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 contemptible</td><td>/kənˈtemptəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可鄙的，卑劣的</td><td>contemptible behavior<br>卑劣的行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cheating a blind beggar is a contemptible thing to do.  欺骗盲丐是可鄙的行为。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 infamous</td><td>/ˈɪnfəməs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>臭名昭著的</td><td>an infamous crime<br>臭名昭著的罪行</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dictator remains infamous for the brutal purges he ordered during his rule.  这位独裁者因其统治期间下令进行的残酷清洗而臭名昭著。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 censurable</del></td><td>/ˈsɛnʃərəbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>该受谴责的</td><td>censurable behavior<br>该受谴责的行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The committee called his handling of the funds clearly censurable.  委员会称他对资金的处理明显应受谴责。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 discreditable</td><td>/dɪsˈkrɛdɪtəbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不光彩的，可耻的</td><td>discreditable conduct<br>不光彩的行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The committee found his discreditable conduct unworthy of a public official.  委员会认为他不光彩的行为不配做公职人员。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 illaudable</del></td><td>/ɪˈlɔdəbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不值得称赞的</td><td>an illaudable motive<br>不值称赞的动机</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics judged his illaudable silence during the crisis a moral failure.  批评者认为他危机中不值称赞的沉默是道德失败。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 reprehensible</td><td>/ˌreprɪˈhensəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>应受谴责的，可鄙的</td><td>morally reprehensible<br>道德上应受谴责</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Lying to the families was reprehensible and utterly unnecessary.  对那些家庭撒谎既可鄙又完全没有必要。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>estrange</b></td><td>/ɪˈstreɪndʒ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使疏远，离间，使感情失和</td><td>estrange the two brothers<br>离间两兄弟</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A long quarrel over money estranged the two brothers for years.  一场长久的金钱纠纷使两兄弟多年感情失和。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 alienate</td><td>/ˈeɪliəneɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>疏远，离间；使疏离</td><td>alienate one's supporters<br>疏远自己的支持者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His harsh remarks alienated many longtime friends within a single evening.  他刻薄的言辞在一个晚上就疏远了许多老朋友。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 disaffect</del></td><td>/ˌdɪsəˈfekt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使不满，使离心</td><td>disaffected voters<br>心生不满的选民</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The broken promises disaffected much of the party base.  这些空头承诺使党的许多基础支持者离心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 disgruntle</del></td><td>/dɪsˈɡrʌntl/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使不满，使发怒</td><td>disgruntle the staff<br>使员工不满</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The sudden pay freeze disgruntled the entire department.  突然的薪资冻结让整个部门都不满。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sour</td><td>/ˈsaʊər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词、形容词</td><td>使恶化，使变坏；酸的</td><td>sour the relationship<br>使关系恶化</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The argument soured their friendship for years to come.  那次争吵使他们的友谊多年蒙上阴影。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 reconcile</td><td>/ˈrekənsaɪl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使和解，协调</td><td>reconcile conflicting views<br>调和相互冲突的观点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It is hard to reconcile these two versions of the story.  很难把这个故事的两种说法协调一致。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>etch</b></td><td>/etʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>留下深刻印象，铭记</td><td>etched in my memory<br>铭刻在我记忆中</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That afternoon is etched in my memory more clearly than yesterday.  那个下午比昨天更清晰地铭刻在我记忆中。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 impress</td><td>/ɪmˈpres/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使铭记；使钦佩</td><td>impress upon his mind<br>铭刻在他脑中</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His father impressed on him the value of finishing every job.  父亲让他牢记做完每件事的价值。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 imprint</td><td>/ɪmˈprɪnt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>留下印记，铭刻；印记</td><td>imprint on the mind<br>铭刻于心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The melody was imprinted on her memory after a single hearing.  那段旋律听一遍就铭刻在她记忆里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 infix</td><td>/ɪnˈfɪks/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>使铭记，插入</td><td>infix a habit deeply<br>深深植入一种习惯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years of drill infixed the routine so deeply that he could perform it half asleep.  多年操练把这套流程深植于他心中，他半睡半醒都能做出来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ingrain</td><td>/ɪnˈɡreɪn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使根深蒂固，深植</td><td>ingrain a sense of duty<br>把责任感深植于心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years of training ingrained in her the habit of checking every figure twice.  多年训练使她养成了把每个数字复查两遍的习惯。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 4

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>eternal</b></td><td>/ɪˈtɜːrnl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>永恒的</td><td>eternal life<br>永生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The poem promises the beloved eternal youth.  这首诗许诺爱人永恒的青春。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ageless</td><td>/ˈeɪdʒləs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>永不显老的，永恒的</td><td>ageless beauty<br>不老的美貌</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her ageless beauty surprised everyone at the reunion.  她不显岁月的美貌让聚会上的人都惊讶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 everlasting</td><td>/ˌevərˈlæstɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>永恒的，持久的</td><td>everlasting peace<br>永久的和平</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They vowed everlasting loyalty to each other.  他们发誓永远彼此忠诚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 immortal</td><td>/ɪˈmɔːrtəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>不朽的，永生的</td><td>immortal fame<br>不朽的声名</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The poet's immortal lines are still quoted two thousand years later.  这位诗人不朽的诗句两千年后仍被引用。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 imperishable</td><td>/ɪmˈperɪʃəbl/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不朽的，永恒的</td><td>imperishable glory<br>不朽的荣耀</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The soldiers won imperishable glory defending the bridge for three days.  士兵们守桥三天赢得了不朽的荣耀。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 perennial</td><td>/pəˈreniəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>长期的，反复出现的</td><td>a perennial problem<br>长期存在的问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Traffic is a perennial problem in this old city.  交通是这座老城长期存在的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 perpetual</td><td>/pərˈpetʃuəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>永恒的，不断的</td><td>perpetual motion<br>永恒运动，永动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He lives in perpetual fear of losing his job.  他一直生活在失业的恐惧中。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 undying</td><td>/ʌnˈdaɪɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>永恒的，不灭的</td><td>undying love<br>永恒的爱</td></tr>
