<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their extravagant spending on parties left the charity nearly broke.  他们在宴会上的挥霍开支让这家慈善机构几乎破产。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 intolerable</td><td>/ɪnˈtɑːlərəbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无法容忍的，难以承受的</td><td>intolerable noise<br>无法容忍的噪音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The heat in that attic room became intolerable by early afternoon.  下午刚过，那间阁楼里的暑热就变得无法忍受。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lavish</td><td>/ˈlævɪʃ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>奢侈的，大量的，无节制的；挥霍，浪费</td><td>a lavish banquet<br>奢华的宴会</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They hosted a lavish banquet for three hundred guests in the garden.  他们在花园里为三百位客人举办了一场奢华的宴会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 overdue</td><td>/ˌoʊvərˈduː/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>过期的，逾期的；早该发生的</td><td>an overdue payment<br>逾期的付款</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The library fined her for three books that were badly overdue.  图书馆因三本严重逾期的书对她罚款。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 overweening</td><td>/ˌoʊvərˈwiːnɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>傲慢的，专横的</td><td>overweening pride<br>过度的骄傲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His overweening confidence led him to ignore every warning from the engineers.  他过度的自信使他忽视了工程师的每一次警告。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unconscionable</td><td>/ʌnˈkɑːnʃənəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>过分的，不合理的；不道德的</td><td>unconscionable delay<br>过分的拖延</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Charging that fee for a five-minute repair seems unconscionable to me.  五分钟的维修收那笔费用在我看来太过分。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 middling</td><td>/ˈmɪdlɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>中等的，一般的</td><td>middling quality<br>中等的质量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The restaurant serves middling food at prices that feel a bit high.  这家餐厅食物一般，价格却偏高。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 moderate</td><td>/ˈmɑːdəreɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词、形容词</td><td>缓和，减轻；适度的</td><td>moderate one's demands<br>降低自己的要求</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He agreed to moderate his tone during the debate.  他同意在辩论中缓和语气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 modest</td><td>/ˈmɑːdəst/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>谦虚的；不大的，适度的</td><td>modest about her success<br>对成功很谦虚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He stayed modest about his award and thanked his team.  他对自己的奖项很谦虚，还感谢了团队。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 reasonable</td><td>/ˈriːzənəbəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>合理的，通情达理的</td><td>a reasonable request<br>合理的请求</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That seems like a perfectly reasonable request.  那似乎是完全合理的要求。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 temperate</td><td>/ˈtempərət/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>（言行举止）有分寸的，有节制的</td><td>a temperate response<br>有分寸的回应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave a temperate reply to a very rude question.  面对无礼的问题她的回答很有分寸。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>exotic</b></td><td>/ɪɡˈzɑːtɪk/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>外来的，不同寻常的</td><td>exotic tropical fruit<br>外来的热带水果</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The market sells exotic tropical fruit flown in each morning.  这个市场出售每天清晨空运来的外来热带水果。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 outlandish</td><td>/aʊtˈlændɪʃ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>古怪的，奇异的</td><td>outlandish conspiracy theories<br>离奇的阴谋论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her outlandish hat made every head turn as she entered the hall.  她走进大厅时，那顶古怪的帽子让所有人回头。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 strange</td><td>/ˈstreɪndʒ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>奇怪的；陌生的</td><td>a strange noise upstairs<br>楼上奇怪的声响</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There was a strange smell coming from the kitchen last night.  昨晚厨房里传出一股奇怪的味道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 indigenous</td><td>/ɪnˈdɪdʒənəs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>土产的，本地的；与生俱来的，先天的</td><td>indigenous plants of the region<br>该地区的本土植物</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Many indigenous species of the island cannot survive anywhere else on the planet.  这个岛上许多本土物种无法在地球其他任何地方存活。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 familiar</td><td>/fəˈmɪljər/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>熟悉的，通晓的</td><td>familiar with the subject<br>熟悉这门学科</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her voice sounded strangely familiar over the phone.  她的声音在电话里听起来奇怪地熟悉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 nonglamorous</del></td><td>/ˌnɑnˈɡlæmərəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不迷人的，平淡无奇的</td><td>nonglamorous daily work<br>平淡无奇的工作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Most research is nonglamorous work involving spreadsheets and long quiet afternoons.  多数研究是涉及表格和漫长安静下午的平淡工作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 plain-jane</td><td>/ˌpleɪn ˈdʒeɪn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>平淡无奇的，不起眼的</td><td>a plain-jane design<br>朴素平淡的设计</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She drove a plain-jane sedan that nobody ever looked at twice.  她开一辆没人会多看一眼的普通轿车。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 unexotic</del></td><td>/ˌʌnɪɡˈzɑtɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不异域的，平常的</td><td>unexotic local food<br>平常的本地食物</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The menu was deliberately unexotic to please cautious family diners.  菜单故意不猎奇，以取悦谨慎的家庭客人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>expansive</b></td><td>/ɪkˈspænsɪv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>话多的，健谈的</td><td>in an expansive mood<br>谈兴正浓</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After dinner he grew expansive and told stories all evening.  晚饭后他变得健谈，整晚讲故事。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 communicative</td><td>/kəˈmjuːnɪkətɪv/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>健谈的，愿意交流的</td><td>a communicative host<br>健谈的主人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He grew more communicative after the second cup of strong tea.  喝下第二杯浓茶后他变得更爱说话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 eloquent</td><td>/ˈeləkwənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>雄辩的，有说服力的</td><td>an eloquent speech<br>雄辩的演讲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His eloquent defense of the plan won several extra votes.  他对该计划雄辩的辩护多赢得了几张选票。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 garrulous</td><td>/ˈɡærələs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>啰嗦的，话多令人厌烦的</td><td>a garrulous neighbor<br>唠叨的邻居</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Our garrulous neighbor talked for an hour about his lawn.  我们唠叨的邻居就他的草坪讲了一个小时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 glib</td><td>/ɡlɪb/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>油腔滑调的；肤浅的</td><td>a glib salesman<br>油腔滑调的销售员</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His glib answers convinced no one on the hiring committee that afternoon.  那天下午他油腔滑调的回答没能说服招聘委员会的任何人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 loquacious</td><td>/ləˈkweɪʃəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>话多的</td><td>a loquacious guide<br>话多的导游</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Our loquacious guide talked steadily from the museum door to the exit.  我们话多的导游从博物馆门口一直讲到出口。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 talkative</td><td>/ˈtɔːkətɪv/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>健谈的，爱说话的</td><td>a talkative passenger<br>健谈的乘客</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Our talkative driver described every building along the winding coastal road.  健谈的司机介绍了蜿蜒海滨路上的每座建筑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vocative</td><td>/ˈvɑkətɪv/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>呼唤的，称呼的；呼格的</td><td>the vocative case<br>呼格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Latin uses a vocative form when directly addressing a person.  拉丁语直接称呼某人时使用呼格形式。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 voluble</td><td>/ˈvɑːljəbl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>健谈的，话多的</td><td>a voluble speaker<br>健谈的讲话者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Our voluble guide talked cheerfully about every stone in the old city.  我们健谈的导游兴致勃勃地讲述古城里的每一块石头。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 laconic</td><td>/ləˈkɑːnɪk/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>简洁（以至于显得粗鲁或难以理解）的</td><td>a laconic reply<br>简短生硬的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His laconic reply told us nothing about what had actually happened.  他简短生硬的回答没有告诉我们实际发生了什么。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 reserved</td><td>/rɪˈzɜːrvd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>内向的，缄默的</td><td>a quiet, reserved man<br>安静内向的男子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her father was a reserved man who rarely spoke about his feelings.  她的父亲是个内向的人，很少谈论自己的感受。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 reticent</td><td>/ˈretɪsnt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>沉默不语的</td><td>reticent about his past<br>对往事讳莫如深</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was oddly reticent about the years he spent working abroad.  他对自己在国外工作的那些年出奇地沉默不语。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 taciturn</td><td>/ˈtæsɪtɜːrn/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>沉默寡言的，话少的</td><td>a taciturn farmer<br>沉默寡言的农夫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The taciturn farmer answered our questions with a nod or a shrug.  沉默寡言的农夫只用点头或耸肩回答我们的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>expedite</b></td><td>/ˈekspədaɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>加快进程</td><td>expedite the process<br>加快流程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Hiring two more clerks expedited the review of every application.  又雇了两名职员加快了每份申请的审核。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 haste</td><td>/ˈheɪst/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>急速，匆忙</td><td>in great haste<br>极为匆忙地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 In her haste she left the tickets on the kitchen table.  她匆忙之中把票忘在了厨房桌上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 celerity</td><td>/səˈlerəti/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>迅速，敏捷</td><td>act with celerity<br>迅速行动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The crew unloaded the ship with a celerity that impressed everyone.  船员卸货之迅速令所有人赞叹。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dispatch</td><td>/dɪˈspætʃ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>迅速；发送，派遣</td><td>with great dispatch<br>极为迅速地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The courier handled the urgent order with great dispatch and no complaints.  快递员极为迅速地处理了这份紧急订单，毫无怨言。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 retard</td><td>/rɪˈtɑːrd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>减速，延迟</td><td>retard the growth<br>延缓生长</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Language barriers retarded the progress of the peace negotiations.  语言障碍阻碍了和平谈判的进展。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 foot-dragging</td><td>/ˈfʊt ˌdræɡɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>故意拖延，磨蹭</td><td>bureaucratic foot-dragging<br>官僚的拖延</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Months of foot-dragging delayed the repairs until the roof finally collapsed.  数月的拖延使维修一再延后，屋顶最终塌了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>expediency</b></td><td>/ɪkˈspiːdiənsi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>应急手段，权宜之计</td><td>political expediency<br>政治上的权宜之计</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The truce was a matter of political expediency, not principle.  这次休战是政治上的权宜之计，而非出于原则。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 advisability</td><td>/ədˌvaɪzəˈbɪləti/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>适当，明智；可取性</td><td>question the advisability<br>质疑其可取性</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The board debated the advisability of opening a branch abroad.  董事会讨论在海外开分支是否可取。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 desirability</td><td>/dɪˌzaɪrəˈbɪləti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>可取性，合意程度</td><td>the desirability of reform<br>改革的可取性</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Few doubted the desirability of cleaner air, only the cost involved.  很少有人怀疑清洁空气的可取性，只质疑成本。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 judiciousness</del></td><td>/dʒuːˈdɪʃəsnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>明智，审慎</td><td>the judiciousness of the plan<br>计划的明智</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her judiciousness in handling complaints kept small problems from growing large.  她处理投诉的审慎让小问题没有变大。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 makeshift</td><td>/ˈmeɪkˌʃɪft/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>临时替代的，权宜的</td><td>a makeshift shelter<br>临时的住所</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Volunteers built a makeshift clinic from tents and folding tables.  志愿者用帐篷和折叠桌搭起临时诊所。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 imprudence</td><td>/ˌɪmˈpruːdəns/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>轻率，不谨慎</td><td>an act of imprudence<br>一次轻率的行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His imprudence with money ruined the small company.  他在金钱上的轻率毁了这家小公司。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 inadvisability</del></td><td>/ˌɪnədˌvaɪzəˈbɪləti/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>不妥当，不可取</td><td>the inadvisability of delay<br>拖延之不可取</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Engineers stressed the inadvisability of reopening the bridge before full inspection.  工程师强调未全面检查就重开大桥不可取。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 inexpediency</del></td><td>/ˌɪnɪkˈspiːdiənsi/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>不适宜，不明智</td><td>the inexpediency of haste<br>匆忙之不明智</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Later events proved the inexpediency of announcing the merger so early.  后来的事证明这么早宣布合并并不明智。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 injudiciousness</del></td><td>/ˌɪndʒuːˈdɪʃəsnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>不明智，欠考虑</td><td>obvious injudiciousness<br>明显的欠考虑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His injudiciousness in emailing the whole company cost him the promotion.  他给全公司群发邮件的欠考虑使他失去晋升。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>expiate</b></td><td>/ˈekspieɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>赎罪，纠正</td><td>expiate his guilt<br>赎他的罪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He spent years doing charity work to expiate his guilt.  他做了多年慈善工作来赎自己的罪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mend</td><td>/ˈmend/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>纠正，改正；修补</td><td>mend his ways<br>改正他的行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He promised to mend his ways after the second stern warning.  第二次严厉警告后他承诺改正。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 redeem</td><td>/rɪˈdiːm/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>改过自新</td><td>redeem oneself through service<br>以服务改过自新</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He hoped to redeem himself through years of quiet public service.  他希望通过多年默默的公共服务来改过自新。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 atone for</td><td>/əˈtoʊn fɔr/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>为…赎罪，补偿</td><td>atone for past mistakes<br>弥补过去的错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He spent years volunteering to atone for the harm he caused.  他多年做志愿者以弥补自己造成的伤害。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 8

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>expire</b></td><td>/ɪkˈspaɪər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>断气，死亡；到期</td><td>the contract expires<br>合同到期</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Your passport expires next month, so apply for a new one.  你的护照下个月到期，所以要申请新的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 conclude</td><td>/kənˈkluːd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>结束；得出结论</td><td>conclude the ceremony<br>结束仪式</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge concluded that the evidence was too weak.  法官得出结论认为证据太薄弱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 elapse</td><td>/ɪˈlæps/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>（时间）流逝，过去</td><td>years elapsed quietly<br>岁月静静流逝</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Three weeks elapsed before anyone noticed the missing files.  三周过去才有人注意到文件不见了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 terminate</td><td>/ˈtɜːrməˌneɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>终止，结束</td><td>terminate the agreement<br>终止协议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The club terminated his membership without any explanation.  俱乐部未加解释就终止了他的会员资格。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 decease</td><td>/dɪˈsiːs/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>死亡，去世</td><td>since his decease<br>自他去世以来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The estate was divided among the children after her decease.  她去世后遗产在子女间分割。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 leave off</td><td>/liːv ˈɔf/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>停止，中断</td><td>leave off working<br>停止工作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We left off reading at the chapter where the storm begins.  我们在暴风雨开始的那章停下阅读。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 let up</td><td>/lɛt ˈʌp/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>减弱，停止</td><td>the rain let up<br>雨停了</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The rain finally let up just before the parade was cancelled.  游行取消前雨终于停了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 discontinue</td><td>/dɪskənˈtɪnju/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>停止，中断</td><td>discontinue the product line<br>停产该产品线</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The brand discontinued its cheapest model last year.  该品牌去年停产了最便宜的型号。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 end</td><td>/ˈend/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>结束；末端</td><td>end the war<br>结束战争</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The strike ended after both sides agreed on wages.  双方就工资达成一致后罢工结束。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 finish</td><td>/ˈfɪnɪʃ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>完成，结束</td><td>finish the report tonight<br>今晚完成报告</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She hopes to finish her thesis before the winter holiday.  她希望在寒假前完成论文。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wind up</td><td>/waɪnd ˈʌp/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>结束，终结；上紧发条</td><td>wind up the meeting<br>结束会议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They wound up the project a month ahead of the deadline.  他们比截止期提前一个月结束了项目。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wink out</td><td>/wɪŋk ˈaʊt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>熄灭，消失</td><td>the lights winked out<br>灯光熄灭</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 One by one the harbor lights winked out before the dawn.  港口的灯在黎明前一盏盏熄灭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 come to life</td><td>/kʌm tuː ˈlaɪf/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>苏醒，活跃起来</td><td>come to life again<br>重新活跃起来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The sleepy market comes to life the moment the fishing boats return.  渔船一回来，冷清的市场立刻活跃起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 continue</td><td>/kənˈtɪnjuː/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>继续，持续</td><td>continue without pause<br>不停地持续</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The negotiations continued for eleven hours without a proper break.  谈判持续了十一小时没有正式休息。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 persist</td><td>/pərˈsɪst/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>持续存在；坚持</td><td>the symptoms persist<br>症状持续</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Fog persisted over the runway until late in the afternoon.  雾在跑道上空持续到下午晚些时候。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 hang on</td><td>/hæŋ ˈɑn/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>坚持，持续；稍等</td><td>hang on a little longer<br>再坚持一会儿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old bridge hung on for decades after engineers condemned it.  那座旧桥在被判定危险后又存留数十年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>explicit</b></td><td>/ɪkˈsplɪsɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>表达清晰的，明确的</td><td>explicit instructions<br>明确的指示</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave explicit instructions about how to store the samples.  她就样品的保存方式给出了明确指示。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 clear-cut</td><td>/ˌklɪrˈkʌt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>明确的，清晰的</td><td>a clear-cut decision<br>明确的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The evidence gave us a clear-cut answer for the first time.  证据第一次给了我们明确的答案。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 definite</td><td>/ˈdefənət/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>明确的，确定的</td><td>a definite answer<br>明确的答复</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We need a definite answer before we can book the venue.  我们需要明确答复才能订场地。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 specific</td><td>/spəˈsɪfɪk/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明确的，特定的；专门的</td><td>a specific example<br>一个具体的例子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Can you give me a specific example of the problem?  你能给我一个这个问题的具体例子吗？</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unambiguous</td><td>/ˌʌnæmˈbɪɡjuəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>明确的，毫不含糊的</td><td>unambiguous written instructions<br>明确的书面指示</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The contract gives unambiguous rules about late payment.  合同就迟付款给出了明确规定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unequivocal</td><td>/ˌʌnɪˈkwɪvəkəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>毫不含糊的，明确的</td><td>an unequivocal denial<br>毫不含糊的否认</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The board issued an unequivocal statement supporting the director.  董事会发表了明确支持主任的声明。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 univocal</del></td><td>/juːˈnɪvəkəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>含义明确的</td><td>a univocal statement<br>含义明确的陈述</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The treaty needs univocal wording that no side can reinterpret later.  这份条约需要含义明确的措辞，任何一方都无法事后曲解。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 obscure</td><td>/əbˈskjʊr/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>含义模糊的；平凡的，不知名的</td><td>an obscure poet<br>不知名的诗人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He remained an obscure poet until a critic rediscovered his work decades later.  他一直是个不知名的诗人，直到几十年后一位批评家重新发现他的作品。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 implicit</td><td>/ɪmˈplɪsɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不言而喻的，心照不宣的</td><td>an implicit agreement<br>心照不宣的协议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Club members share an implicit agreement to split all travel costs.  俱乐部成员之间有一个心照不宣的约定：平摊所有旅行开销。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 implied</td><td>/ɪmˈplaɪd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>暗示的，含蓄的</td><td>an implied threat<br>暗含的威胁</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His polite note carried an implied threat that everyone recognized.  他礼貌的字条里含着人人都听得出的威胁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 inferred</td><td>/ɪnˈfɜːrd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>推断出的，间接得出的</td><td>an inferred conclusion<br>推断出的结论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The date is inferred from tree rings rather than written records.  这个年代是从树轮而非文献推断出来的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ambiguous</td><td>/æmˈbɪɡjuəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>模糊的，含义不明的</td><td>an ambiguous reply<br>含糊其辞的答复</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The contract's wording is ambiguous and invites future disputes.  合同措辞含义不明，容易引发日后争端。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 circuitous</td><td>/sərˈkjuːɪtəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>迂回的，不直接的</td><td>a circuitous route<br>迂回的路线</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We took a circuitous route to avoid the flooded highway.  我们绕了一条迂回的路以避开被淹的公路。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 equivocal</td><td>/ɪˈkwɪvəkəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>模棱两可的，含糊的</td><td>an equivocal answer<br>模棱两可的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The witness gave an equivocal account of that evening.  证人对那晚的叙述模棱两可。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 indefinite</td><td>/ɪnˈdefənət/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不明确的，含糊的；无限期的</td><td>an indefinite answer<br>含糊的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They postponed the trip for an indefinite period without explaining why.  他们无限期推迟了旅行，也没解释原因。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unspecific</td><td>/ˌʌnspəˈsɪfɪk/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不具体的，笼统的</td><td>unspecific complaints<br>笼统的抱怨</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The warning was so unspecific that nobody knew which road to avoid.  警告太笼统，没人知道该避开哪条路。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 vague</td><td>/veɪɡ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>表达不清的</td><td>a vague answer<br>含糊的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His vague answer told the reporters almost nothing about the plan.  他含糊的回答几乎没有向记者透露任何计划内容。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 inchoate</td><td>/ɪnˈkoʊət/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>新生的，才开始的</td><td>inchoate ideas<br>尚未成形的想法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His inchoate plans changed every time he described them to us.  他那些尚未成形的计划每次向我们描述时都变了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>exploit</b></td><td>/ɪkˈsplɔɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>最大程度地利用</td><td>exploit natural resources<br>开发自然资源</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company plans to exploit the island's natural resources responsibly.  公司计划负责任地开发这个岛的自然资源。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 abuse</td><td>/əˈbjuːz/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>辱骂，抨击；不正当或不合理使用</td><td>abuse of power<br>滥用权力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was accused of abusing his position for profit.  他被指控滥用职位谋利。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 leverage</td><td>/ˈlevərɪdʒ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>影响力，筹码；杠杆</td><td>gain more leverage<br>获得更多筹码</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The union used the strike to gain leverage in talks.  工会利用罢工在谈判中取得影响力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 play on</td><td>/pleɪ ˈɑn/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>利用（他人情感）</td><td>play on their fears<br>利用他们的恐惧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The advertisement plays on parents' fears about their children's safety.  广告利用父母对孩子安全的恐惧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 capitalize on</td><td>/ˈkæpɪtəlaɪz ɑn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>利用，从…中获益</td><td>capitalize on the chance<br>利用这个机会</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The team capitalized on a defensive mistake to score twice.  球队利用一次防守失误连进两球。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 impose on</td><td>/ɪmˈpoʊz ɑn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>利用，占…便宜；打扰</td><td>impose on his kindness<br>利用他的好心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I hate to impose on you, but could I borrow the car tonight?  我不想麻烦你，但今晚能借下车吗？</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>exponent</b></td><td>/ɪkˈspoʊnənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>倡导者，代表人物；指数</td><td>a leading exponent<br>主要的倡导者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He became a leading exponent of modern jazz in the fifties.  他在五十年代成为现代爵士乐的主要倡导者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 advocator</del></td><td>/ˈædvəkeɪtər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>拥护者，提倡者</td><td>an advocator of reform<br>改革的提倡者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She became a tireless advocator of free school meals for children.  她成了儿童免费校餐不倦的提倡者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 backer</td><td>/ˈbækər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>支持者，资助人</td><td>a wealthy backer<br>富有的资助人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The play closed when its main backer withdrew his funding.  主要资助人撤资后这部剧停演了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 booster</td><td>/ˈbuːstər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>支持者，鼓吹者；助推器</td><td>a booster of local business<br>本地商业的鼓吹者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was the town's loudest booster long before tourists discovered it.  游客发现这里之前他就是全镇最响的鼓吹者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 champion</td><td>/ˈtʃæmpiən/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>支持</td><td>champion a cause<br>支持一项事业</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She has championed the rights of migrant workers for years.  她多年来一直为农民工的权益奔走呼吁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 espouser</del></td><td>/ɪˈspaʊzər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>支持者，拥护者</td><td>an espouser of the cause<br>该事业的拥护者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 As an early espouser of the theory, he faced constant ridicule.  作为该理论的早期拥护者，他不断受到嘲笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 friend</td><td>/ˈfrend/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>朋友，友人</td><td>a close friend<br>亲密的朋友</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She has been my closest friend since primary school.  她从小学起就是我最亲密的朋友。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 promoter</td><td>/prəˈmoʊtər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>支持者，倡导者；主办人</td><td>a promoter of literacy<br>识字运动的倡导者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The concert promoter guaranteed the band a fee regardless of ticket sales.  演出主办方无论票房如何都保证付给乐队报酬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 proponent</td><td>/prəˈpoʊnənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>建议者，支持者</td><td>a proponent of change<br>变革的支持者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She became the leading proponent of change within the old institution.  她成了这家老机构内部倡导变革的主要支持者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 adversary</td><td>/ˈædvərseri/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>敌手，对手</td><td>a formidable adversary<br>强劲的对手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He respected his adversary even while fighting to win.  即使在拼命求胜时，他也尊重自己的对手。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 antagonist</td><td>/ænˈtæɡənɪst/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>对手，敌人</td><td>a bitter antagonist<br>势不两立的对手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her chief antagonist in the debate was a former professor.  她在辩论中的主要对手是一位前教授。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 opponent</td><td>/əˈpoʊnənt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>对手，反对者</td><td>a worthy opponent<br>值得尊敬的对手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She defeated a much stronger opponent in the final round.  她在决赛中击败了一位强得多的对手。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>expurgate</b></del></td><td>/ˈekspərɡeɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>净化（书等），删去（不当处）</td><td>expurgate a text<br>删改文本</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Editors expurgated several passages before the book reached young readers.  编辑在这本书交到小读者手中之前删去了几个段落。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 bowdlerize</del></td><td>/ˈboʊdləraɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>删改（书中不当处），净化</td><td>bowdlerize a classic<br>删改一部名著</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Victorian editors bowdlerized the play before letting children read it.  维多利亚时代的编者在让孩子阅读前删改了这部剧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 obliterate</td><td>/əˈblɪtəreɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>抹去；彻底消灭</td><td>obliterate all traces of it<br>抹去它的一切痕迹</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The March blizzard obliterated all traces of the early spring flowers.  三月的暴风雪抹去了早春花朵的一切痕迹。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 launder</td><td>/ˈlɔːndər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>洗钱；清洗，净化</td><td>launder dirty money<br>洗黑钱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They laundered the profits through a chain of small restaurants.  他们通过一连串小餐馆洗白利润。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 red-pencil</td><td>/ˌred ˈpensəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>删改，删除</td><td>red-pencil the report<br>删改报告</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The editor red-penciled every sentence that might offend advertisers.  编辑删掉了每一句可能得罪广告商的话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 clean up</td><td>/ˌkliːn ˈʌp/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>清理；删去不雅内容</td><td>clean up the script<br>净化剧本内容</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They cleaned up the lyrics before playing the song on radio.  他们在电台播放前净化了歌词。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>exquisite</b></td><td>/ɪkˈskwɪzɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>精致精巧的</td><td>exquisite workmanship<br>精致的工艺</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The tiny box showed exquisite workmanship in every carved detail.  这个小盒子每一处雕刻细节都显示出精致的工艺。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dainty</td><td>/ˈdeɪnti/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>精致的，小巧的；讲究的</td><td>a dainty little cup<br>精致的小杯子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She wore a dainty pair of silk gloves to the evening reception.  她戴着一副精致的丝质手套去参加晚间招待会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 delicate</td><td>/ˈdeləkət/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>微妙的，精细的；脆弱的</td><td>a delicate distinction<br>微妙的区别</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She has a delicate ear for the subtle differences between dialects.  她对方言之间的细微差别有很灵敏的耳朵。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 elegant</td><td>/ˈeləɡənt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>优雅的，精致的</td><td>an elegant solution<br>优雅的解决方案</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The hotel lobby was decorated in an elegant and understated style.  酒店大堂以优雅内敛的风格装饰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 recherché</del></td><td>/reɪˈʃɛrʃeɪ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>精选的；讲究而罕见的</td><td>recherché tastes<br>讲究的品味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His recherché vocabulary impressed some readers and annoyed others.  他罕见讲究的用词让一些读者赞叹，也让另一些人厌烦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 select</td><td>/səˈlekt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>精选的，上等的；挑选</td><td>a select group of guests<br>精选的一小群客人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only a select few were invited to the private tasting.  只有精选的少数人受邀参加私人品鉴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>extant</b></td><td>/ekˈstænt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>现存的</td><td>the earliest extant manuscript<br>现存最早的手稿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only three copies of the manuscript are still extant today.  这份手稿如今只有三个抄本现存。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 current</td><td>/ˈkɜːrənt/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>现在的，通行的；水流，电流</td><td>current practice<br>现行做法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The current version of the software fixes several old bugs.  该软件的现行版本修复了若干旧漏洞。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 immediate</td><td>/ɪˈmiːdiət/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>紧急的；立即的</td><td>an immediate need<br>迫切的需要</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The village has an immediate need for clean drinking water.  这个村子迫切需要干净的饮用水。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ongoing</td><td>/ˈɑːnˌɡoʊɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>持续进行的</td><td>an ongoing investigation<br>持续进行的调查</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The repairs are ongoing and should finish by next month.  维修仍在持续进行，应在下月完成。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 present-day</td><td>/ˌprɛzənt ˈdeɪ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>当今的，现今的</td><td>present-day standards<br>当今的标准</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Present-day readers may find the old spelling confusing.  当今的读者可能觉得旧拼写令人困惑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 existent</td><td>/ɪɡˈzɪstənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>存在的，现存的</td><td>the existent records<br>现存的记录</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only two existent copies of that pamphlet remain in the archive.  档案馆里只剩那本小册子的两份现存副本。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 destroyed</td><td>/dɪˈstrɔɪd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>被毁的，被摧毁的</td><td>a destroyed temple<br>被毁的神庙</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Most of the destroyed documents could never be recovered.  大部分被毁的文件再也无法复原。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 extinct</td><td>/ɪkˈstɪŋkt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>灭绝的</td><td>an extinct species<br>已灭绝的物种</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dodo became extinct less than a century after sailors reached the island.  水手到达该岛不到一百年，渡渡鸟就灭绝了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 lost</td><td>/ˈlɔːst/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>失传的，遗失的</td><td>a lost language<br>失传的语言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Scholars still hope to recover the lost chapters of the epic.  学者们仍希望找回这部史诗失传的篇章。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 missing</td><td>/ˈmɪsɪŋ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>缺失的，失踪的</td><td>the missing pages<br>缺失的页面</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two pages are missing from the only surviving copy.  唯一留存的副本缺失了两页。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dead</td><td>/ˈded/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>死的；毫无生气的</td><td>a dead town at night<br>夜里死寂的小镇</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The resort is completely dead in the winter months.  这个度假地在冬季几个月里毫无生气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 nonextant</del></td><td>/ˌnɑnˈɛkstənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不复存在的，已失传的</td><td>a nonextant edition<br>已失传的版本</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The play is nonextant, known only from a brief ancient description.  这部戏剧已不复存在，只从一段古代简述中得知。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>extemporize</b></del></td><td>/ɪkˈstempəraɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>即兴发言，即兴创作</td><td>extemporize a speech<br>即兴发表演讲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Asked to speak, the mayor extemporized a short but moving speech.  被请上台后，市长即兴发表了一篇简短而动人的演讲。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 follow a script</td><td>/ˈfɑloʊ ə ˈskrɪpt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>照剧本讲，按稿子来</td><td>follow a script closely<br>严格按稿子来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rather than follow a script, the host improvised the whole interview.  主持人没有按稿子来，而是即兴完成了整场访谈。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 planned</td><td>/ˈplænd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>计划好的，有计划的</td><td>a carefully planned trip<br>精心策划的旅行</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The whole ceremony was planned down to the last detail.  整个典礼被计划到了最后一个细节。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>extenuate</b></del></td><td>/ɪkˈstenjueɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>减轻罪过</td><td>extenuate the offense<br>减轻罪责</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His youth did not extenuate so serious an offense.  他年纪轻并不能减轻如此严重的罪责。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 deodorize</del></td><td>/diˈoʊdəraɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>除臭，去除气味</td><td>deodorize the room<br>给房间除臭</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They deodorized the kitchen after the fish had spoiled.  鱼变质后他们给厨房除了臭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 excuse</td><td>/ɪkˈskjuːz/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>为…辩解，宽恕；借口</td><td>excuse his rudeness<br>为他的粗鲁辩解</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Nothing can excuse such careless handling of the evidence.  如此草率地处理证据是无法辩解的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 explain away</td><td>/ɪkˈspleɪn əˈweɪ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>为…开脱，辩解掉</td><td>explain away the discrepancy<br>把差异辩解过去</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He tried to explain away the missing money as a bookkeeping error.  他试图把丢失的钱辩解为记账失误。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gloss over</td><td>/ɡlɔs ˈoʊvər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>掩饰，含糊带过</td><td>gloss over the problems<br>对问题轻描淡写</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report glossed over the losses of the previous year.  报告对前一年的亏损轻描淡写。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 gloze over</del></td><td>/ɡloʊz ˈoʊvər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>掩饰，粉饰</td><td>gloze over the flaws<br>掩饰缺陷</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report glozes over the failures of the previous administration.  该报告掩饰了上届政府的失败。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>extinct</b></td><td>/ɪkˈstɪŋkt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>灭绝的</td><td>an extinct species<br>已灭绝的物种</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dodo became extinct less than a century after sailors reached the island.  水手到达该岛不到一百年，渡渡鸟就灭绝了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bygone</td><td>/ˈbaɪˌɡɔːn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>过去的，已往的</td><td>a bygone era<br>逝去的年代</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mansion still recalls the elegance of a bygone age.  这座宅邸仍让人想起逝去年代的优雅。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 bypast</del></td><td>/ˌbaɪˈpæst/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>过去的，往昔的</td><td>bypast days<br>往昔的日子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She spoke fondly of bypast summers on her grandfather's farm.  她深情地谈起祖父农场上往昔的夏天。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 defunct</td><td>/dɪˈfʌŋkt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不再存在的，已废止的</td><td>a defunct company<br>已倒闭的公司</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The rule has been defunct for over twenty years.  这条规定已废止二十多年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 expired</td><td>/ɪkˈspaɪrd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>过期的，失效的</td><td>an expired passport<br>过期的护照</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pharmacy discarded all the expired medicines.  药房丢掉了所有过期药品。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 nonextant</del></td><td>/ˌnɑnˈɛkstənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不复存在的，已失传的</td><td>a nonextant edition<br>已失传的版本</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The play is nonextant, known only from a brief ancient description.  这部戏剧已不复存在，只从一段古代简述中得知。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vanished</td><td>/ˈvænɪʃt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>消失的，不见了的</td><td>a vanished civilization<br>消失的文明</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Archaeologists study the pottery of that vanished people.  考古学家研究那个消失民族的陶器。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 alive</td><td>/əˈlaɪv/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>活着的，存在的</td><td>keep the tradition alive<br>让传统延续下去</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The language is still alive in a few remote mountain villages.  这门语言在少数偏远山村中仍然活着。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 extant</td><td>/ekˈstænt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>现存的</td><td>the earliest extant manuscript<br>现存最早的手稿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only three copies of the manuscript are still extant today.  这份手稿如今只有三个抄本现存。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 existing</td><td>/ɪɡˈzɪstɪŋ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>现有的，现存的</td><td>existing regulations<br>现有的规定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We can upgrade the existing system instead of buying a new one.  我们可以升级现有系统，而不必新买一套。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 living</td><td>/ˈlɪvɪŋ/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>活着的，在世的</td><td>the greatest living writer<br>在世最伟大的作家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Coelacanths were long thought extinct until a living one was caught.  矛尾鱼长期被认为已灭绝，直到捕到一条活的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 resuscitated</td><td>/rɪˈsʌsɪteɪtɪd/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>被救活的，复苏的</td><td>a resuscitated patient<br>被救活的病人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The resuscitated swimmer was breathing on his own within minutes.  被救活的泳者几分钟内就能自主呼吸。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 perpetuation</td><td>/pərˌpetʃuˈeɪʃən/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>使永存，延续</td><td>the perpetuation of a myth<br>神话的延续</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Such textbooks contribute to the perpetuation of old stereotypes.  这类教材助长了旧有成见的延续。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 9

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>extinguish</b></td><td>/ɪkˈstɪŋɡwɪʃ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>熄灭</td><td>extinguish the fire<br>扑灭大火</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Firefighters worked all night to extinguish the burning warehouse.  消防员整夜工作扑灭燃烧的仓库。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 blanket</td><td>/ˈblæŋkət/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>覆盖以扑灭；毯子</td><td>blanket the flames<br>覆盖扑灭火焰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Firefighters blanketed the burning oil with thick white foam.  消防员用厚厚的白色泡沫覆盖扑灭了燃烧的油。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 douse</td><td>/daʊs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>扑灭（火或灯）；浇透</td><td>douse the flames<br>扑灭火焰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Firefighters worked all night to douse the flames on the upper floors.  消防员整夜奋战，扑灭了高层的火焰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 quench</td><td>/kwentʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>解（渴）；熄灭</td><td>quench one's thirst<br>解渴</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A glass of cold lemonade quickly quenched my desperate thirst.  一杯冰镇柠檬水很快解了我的焦渴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 put out</td><td>/ˈpʊt ˈaʊt/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>使不便，麻烦；扑灭</td><td>put you out<br>麻烦到你</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I hope my early arrival did not put you out too much.  希望我来得早没太麻烦你。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 snuff out</td><td>/snʌf ˈaʊt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>扑灭；终止，毁灭</td><td>snuff out a candle<br>掐灭蜡烛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A single storm can snuff out years of careful planting.  一场风暴就能毁掉多年的精心种植。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ignite</td><td>/ɪɡˈnaɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>点燃，使燃烧；激起（感情）</td><td>ignite public outrage<br>激起公众愤怒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The verdict ignited public outrage across the entire country within a few hours.  判决在几小时内就激起了全国的公众愤怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 rekindle</td><td>/riˈkɪndəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>重新点燃，重新激起</td><td>rekindle interest<br>重新激起兴趣</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The documentary rekindled public interest in deep-sea exploration.  这部纪录片重新激起了公众对深海探索的兴趣。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>extol</b></td><td>/ɪkˈstoʊl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>赞美；吹捧</td><td>extol his virtues<br>赞美他的品德</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Neighbors still extol his kindness during the long winter flood.  邻居们至今仍赞美他在漫长冬季洪水期间的善举。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 carol</td><td>/ˈkærəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>欢快颂唱，歌颂；颂歌</td><td>carol his praises<br>歌颂他的功绩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children caroled cheerfully outside every door on the street.  孩子们在街上每户门前欢快地颂唱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 exalt</td><td>/ɪɡˈzɔːlt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>赞扬；提升（地位）</td><td>exalt to the skies<br>推崇到极点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics exalt her early novels as masterpieces of the era.  评论家将她早期的小说赞为那个时代的杰作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 glorify</td><td>/ˈɡlɔːrəˌfaɪ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>赞美，美化</td><td>glorify war and violence<br>美化战争与暴力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics said the film glorified war instead of questioning it.  批评者说这部电影美化战争而非质疑它。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 laud</td><td>/ˈlɔːd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>赞美，称赞</td><td>laud his courage<br>赞扬他的勇气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics lauded the young pianist for her fearless interpretation.  评论家赞扬这位年轻钢琴家大胆的演绎。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 magnify</td><td>/ˈmæɡnəˌfaɪ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>放大；夸大</td><td>magnify the problem<br>夸大问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lens magnifies the specimen four hundred times.  这个镜头把样本放大四百倍。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 resound</td><td>/rɪˈzaʊnd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>回响；被赞颂</td><td>resound with praise<br>充满赞颂之声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The hall resounded with applause for the young violinist.  大厅回响着对这位年轻小提琴手的掌声。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 censure</td><td>/ˈsenʃər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>公开表示反对，谴责</td><td>a vote of censure<br>谴责性投票</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The council voted to censure the mayor for his conduct.  议会投票谴责市长的行为。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 impugn</td><td>/ɪmˈpjuːn/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>抨击，质疑（诚信、动机）</td><td>impugn someone's motives<br>质疑某人的动机</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He never impugned the honesty of his opponents.  他从不质疑对手的诚实。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 malign</td><td>/məˈlaɪn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>恶意的；诽谤</td><td>malign his motives<br>诋毁他的动机</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rival newspapers began to malign the senator without offering a shred of evidence.  竞争对手的报纸开始诽谤这位参议员，却拿不出一丝证据。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 deprecate</td><td>/ˈdeprəkeɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>不赞成，反对；贬低</td><td>deprecate his efforts<br>贬低他的努力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He tends to deprecate his own achievements whenever anyone praises them.  每当有人称赞，他总倾向于贬低自己的成就。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 detract</td><td>/dɪˈtrækt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>贬低，降低价值；使分心</td><td>detract from the value<br>降低其价值</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The ugly frame detracts from the painting.  难看的画框降低了这幅画的价值。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 rail</td><td>/reɪl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>痛斥，抱怨</td><td>rail against injustice<br>猛烈抨击不公</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Students railed against the unfair new grading policy all week.  学生们整周都在痛斥不公平的新评分政策。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 vilify</td><td>/ˈvɪlɪfaɪ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>诽谤，辱骂</td><td>vilify the whistleblower<br>诽谤举报者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The newspaper tried to vilify the whistleblower instead of examining her evidence.  报纸试图诽谤举报者，而不是审查她的证据。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 excoriate</td><td>/ɪkˈskɔːrieɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>严厉批评；擦伤皮肤</td><td>excoriate the government<br>严厉批评政府</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics excoriated the film for its casual cruelty.  批评家严厉批评该片的随意残忍。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>extort</b></td><td>/ɪkˈstɔːrt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>勒索</td><td>extort money from victims<br>向受害者勒索钱财</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The kidnappers tried to extort a large sum from the victim's family.  绑匪企图从受害者家人那里勒索一大笔钱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wrest</td><td>/rest/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>夺取，强取；费力获得</td><td>wrest control from rivals<br>从对手手中夺取控制权</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The younger managers wrested control of the firm from their rivals.  年轻的经理们从对手手中夺取了公司的控制权。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wring</td><td>/ˈrɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>拧，绞；强行取得</td><td>wring a confession from him<br>从他口中逼出供认</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lawyer wrung an admission from the reluctant witness.  律师从不情愿的证人口中硬逼出一句承认。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>extract</b></td><td>/ɪkˈstrækt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>拔出，抽出；提取</td><td>extract a bad tooth<br>拔掉一颗坏牙</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dentist had to extract a badly decayed back tooth.  牙医不得不拔掉一颗严重腐坏的后牙。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pull</td><td>/ˈpʊl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>拉，拔出</td><td>pull a tooth<br>拔牙</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dentist pulled the broken tooth in a single minute.  牙医一分钟就拔掉了那颗断牙。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 uproot</td><td>/ʌpˈruːt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>根除；使离开家园</td><td>uproot an old olive tree<br>把老橄榄树连根拔起</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The storm uprooted dozens of trees along the coast road.  风暴把沿海公路旁数十棵树连根拔起。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wrest</td><td>/rest/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>夺取，强取；费力获得</td><td>wrest control from rivals<br>从对手手中夺取控制权</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The younger managers wrested control of the firm from their rivals.  年轻的经理们从对手手中夺取了公司的控制权。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wring</td><td>/ˈrɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>拧，绞；强行取得</td><td>wring a confession from him<br>从他口中逼出供认</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lawyer wrung an admission from the reluctant witness.  律师从不情愿的证人口中硬逼出一句承认。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 yank</td><td>/ˈjæŋk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>猛拉，猛拔</td><td>yank the plug out<br>猛地拔出插头</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She yanked the drawer open and grabbed the car keys.  她猛地拉开抽屉，抓起车钥匙。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 root out</td><td>/ˈruːt ˈaʊt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>根除，彻底清除</td><td>root out official corruption<br>根除官场腐败</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new director promised to root out waste in every department.  新主任承诺根除各部门的浪费现象。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tear out</td><td>/tɛr ˈaʊt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>撕下，拽出</td><td>tear out a page<br>撕下一页</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He tore out the map and folded it into his coat pocket.  他撕下地图，折好放进外套口袋。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bloodline</td><td>/ˈblʌdlaɪn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>血统，血脉</td><td>a noble bloodline<br>高贵的血统</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The stallion comes from a famous racing bloodline.  这匹公马出自著名的赛马血统。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 breeding</td><td>/ˈbriːdɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>家世，教养；繁殖</td><td>a man of good breeding<br>有良好教养的人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her manners betrayed careful breeding rather than mere training.  她的举止透出良好家世，而非单纯的训练。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 descent</td><td>/dɪˈsent/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>血统，出身；下降</td><td>of Irish descent<br>爱尔兰血统的</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He is a scientist of Hungarian descent working in Canada.  他是一位在加拿大工作的匈牙利血统科学家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 genealogy</td><td>/ˌdʒiːniˈɑːlədʒi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>家谱，系谱学</td><td>trace one's genealogy<br>追溯家谱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She spent years compiling the genealogy of her mother's family.  她花了数年编纂母亲家族的家谱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lineage</td><td>/ˈlɪniɪdʒ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>血统，世系</td><td>an ancient lineage<br>古老的世系</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The family claims direct lineage from a medieval king.  这个家族自称是某位中世纪国王的直系血脉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 origin</td><td>/ˈɔːrədʒən/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>起源，由来，出身</td><td>the origin of the custom<br>这一习俗的起源</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Scholars still argue about the origin of the strange name.  学者们仍在争论这个奇怪名字的来源。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 family tree</td><td>/ˈfæməli ˈtri/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>家谱，家族树</td><td>draw up a family tree<br>绘制家谱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They pinned a huge family tree to the dining room wall.  他们把一张巨大的家谱钉在餐厅墙上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 embed</td><td>/ɪmˈbed/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>嵌入</td><td>embed a tiny chip<br>嵌入一枚微小芯片</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Engineers embed a tiny chip in each card to store the account data.  工程师在每张卡里嵌入一枚微小芯片来存储账户数据。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>encyclopedic</b></td><td>/ɪnˌsaɪkləˈpiːdɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>知识广博的，包罗万象的</td><td>encyclopedic knowledge<br>广博的知识</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His encyclopedic memory for baseball statistics astonished everyone.  他对棒球数据的百科式记忆令所有人惊讶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 complete</td><td>/kəmˈpliːt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>完全的，彻底的；完成</td><td>a complete failure<br>彻底的失败</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The first attempt was a complete failure.  第一次尝试彻底失败了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 comprehensive</td><td>/ˌkɑːmprɪˈhensɪv/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>全面的，综合的</td><td>a comprehensive survey<br>全面的调查</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The committee produced a comprehensive report on water quality.  委员会就水质提出了一份全面的报告。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 exhaustive</td><td>/ɪɡˈzɔːstɪv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>详尽的，彻底的</td><td>an exhaustive search<br>彻底的搜查</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After an exhaustive review, the board found no wrongdoing.  经过详尽审查，董事会未发现不当行为。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inclusive</td><td>/ɪnˈkluːsɪv/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>包括在内的，包容的</td><td>an inclusive list<br>无所不包的清单</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The fee is inclusive of meals and local transport.  费用包含餐食和当地交通。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 panoramic</td><td>/ˌpænəˈræmɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>全景的</td><td>a panoramic bay view<br>海湾的全景</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 From the rooftop we enjoyed a panoramic view of the harbor at sunset.  从屋顶上我们欣赏到日落时港口的全景。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 thorough</td><td>/ˈθɜːroʊ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>彻底的，详尽的</td><td>a thorough investigation<br>彻底的调查</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Inspectors carried out a thorough search of every storage room.  检查员对每间储藏室都做了彻底搜查。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 universal</td><td>/ˌjuːnəˈvɜːrsəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>普遍的，全体的</td><td>universal human rights<br>普世人权</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Music seems to have a universal appeal across all cultures.  音乐似乎在所有文化中都有普遍的感染力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>extraneous</b></td><td>/ɪkˈstreɪniəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无关的，不重要的</td><td>extraneous information<br>无关的信息</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Please delete all extraneous details from the summary.  请把摘要中所有无关的细节删掉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 impertinent</td><td>/ɪmˈpɜːrtnənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不相关的；无礼的，鲁莽的</td><td>an impertinent remark<br>无礼的言论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lawyer objected that the question was impertinent to the charges.  律师反对说，这个问题与指控无关。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inapplicable</td><td>/ˌɪnəˈplɪkəbəl/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不适用的，不相关的</td><td>inapplicable to this case<br>不适用于本案</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old rule is inapplicable to digital contracts.  旧规则不适用于电子合同。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 irrelative</del></td><td>/ɪˈrɛlətɪv/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无关的，不相干的</td><td>irrelative to the issue<br>与该问题无关</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He dismissed the data as irrelative to his central argument.  他认为这些数据与他的核心论点无关。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 accidental</td><td>/ˌæksɪˈdentl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>意外发生的，偶然的</td><td>an accidental discovery<br>一个偶然的发现</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The fire was accidental, not the result of arson.  这场火灾是意外，不是纵火所致。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 adventitious</td><td>/ˌædvenˈtɪʃəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>外来的，后天的，非内在的</td><td>an adventitious circumstance<br>偶然的外部情况</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her success was not adventitious but the result of years of work.  她的成功不是偶然外来的，而是多年努力的结果。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 external</td><td>/ɪkˈstɜːrnəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>外部的，外来的</td><td>external factors<br>外部因素</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The delay was caused by external factors beyond our control.  延误是由我们无法控制的外部因素造成的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 applicable</td><td>/ˈæplɪkəbl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可用的，可行的；相关的，有关的</td><td>applicable to all students<br>适用于所有学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 This rule is applicable to all students regardless of their year.  这条规定适用于所有学生，不论他们是哪个年级。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 apposite</td><td>/ˈæpəzɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>相关的，合适的</td><td>an apposite comment<br>恰当的评论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She made one apposite comment that settled the whole argument.  她一句恰当的评论就平息了整场争论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 apropos</td><td>/ˌæprəˈpoʊ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词、介词</td><td>相关的；关于，有关</td><td>apropos of nothing<br>毫无来由地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Apropos of nothing, he asked whether I had ever been to Rome.  他毫无来由地问我是否去过罗马。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 germane</td><td>/dʒɜːrˈmeɪn/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有关的，适当的</td><td>germane to the discussion<br>与讨论相关</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her question was germane to the discussion and changed our plan.  她的问题与讨论密切相关，改变了我们的计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pertinent</td><td>/ˈpɜːrtnənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>相关的，恰当的</td><td>pertinent to the discussion<br>与讨论相关的</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only evidence pertinent to the discussion should be presented today.  今天只应提出与讨论相关的证据。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 relative</td><td>/ˈrelətɪv/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>相关的；相对的；亲属</td><td>relative to the topic<br>与该话题相关</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Comfort is relative and depends largely on what you expect.  舒适是相对的，很大程度上取决于你的期望。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 relevant</td><td>/ˈreləvənt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有关系的，重要的</td><td>relevant to the case<br>与本案有关</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge ruled that the old letters were relevant to the case.  法官裁定那些旧信件与本案有关。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>extravagant</b></td><td>/ɪkˈstrævəɡənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>挥霍的；过度的，不必要的</td><td>extravagant spending<br>挥霍的开支</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their extravagant spending on parties left the charity nearly broke.  他们在宴会上的挥霍开支让这家慈善机构几乎破产。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 profligate</td><td>/ˈprɑːflɪɡət/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>挥金如土的，挥霍的</td><td>profligate spending<br>挥霍无度的开支</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The council's profligate spending on ceremonies angered local taxpayers.  议会在典礼上挥霍无度的开支激怒了当地纳税人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 spendthrift</td><td>/ˈspendθrɪft/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>挥霍者，败家子</td><td>a reckless spendthrift<br>肆意挥霍的败家子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A reckless spendthrift, he owed money to nearly every friend.  作为一个挥霍无度的人，他几乎欠了每个朋友的钱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 squandering</td><td>/ˈskwɑːndərɪŋ/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>浪费的，挥霍的</td><td>squandering public funds<br>挥霍公款</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Squandering money on gadgets left him broke by December.  在小玩意上挥霍钱财让他十二月就身无分文。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 thriftless</del></td><td>/ˈθrɪftləs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不节俭的，浪费的</td><td>thriftless habits<br>浪费的习惯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His thriftless spending soon exhausted the family savings.  他不节俭的花销很快耗尽了家庭储蓄。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 unthrifty</del></td><td>/ʌnˈθrɪfti/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不节俭的，挥霍的</td><td>an unthrifty heir<br>挥霍的继承人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The unthrifty young lord sold off the estate piece by piece.  这位挥霍的年轻贵族把地产一块块卖掉了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wasteful</td><td>/ˈweɪstfəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>浪费的，挥霍的</td><td>wasteful of resources<br>浪费资源的</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Printing every email is wasteful and completely unnecessary.  把每封邮件都打印出来既浪费又完全没必要。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 baroque</td><td>/bəˈroʊk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>装饰华丽的，过分雕琢的，复杂的</td><td>baroque ornamentation<br>华丽繁复的装饰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The ceiling was covered with heavy baroque ornamentation in gold.  天花板上覆满了华丽繁复的金色装饰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 exorbitant</td><td>/ɪɡˈzɔːrbɪtənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>过度的</td><td>exorbitant prices<br>过高的价格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Tourists complained about exorbitant prices for bottled water near the temple.  游客抱怨寺庙附近瓶装水价格过高。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 immoderate</td><td>/ɪˈmɑdərət/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无节制的，过度的</td><td>immoderate drinking<br>无节制的饮酒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her immoderate praise made the young writer distinctly uneasy.  她过度的赞美让这位年轻作家明显不安。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inordinate</td><td>/ɪnˈɔːrdənət/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>过度的，过分的</td><td>an inordinate amount of time<br>过多的时间</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He spends an inordinate amount of time arranging his desk.  他花过多时间整理书桌。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lavish</td><td>/ˈlævɪʃ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>奢侈的，大量的，无节制的；挥霍，浪费</td><td>a lavish banquet<br>奢华的宴会</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They hosted a lavish banquet for three hundred guests in the garden.  他们在花园里为三百位客人举办了一场奢华的宴会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 overdue</td><td>/ˌoʊvərˈduː/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>过期的，逾期的；早该发生的</td><td>an overdue payment<br>逾期的付款</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The library fined her for three books that were badly overdue.  图书馆因三本严重逾期的书对她罚款。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 overmuch</td><td>/ˌoʊvərˈmʌtʃ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词、副词</td><td>过多的；过分地</td><td>not worry overmuch<br>不过分担心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She did not worry overmuch about the missed deadline.  她并没有过分担心错过的截止日期。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 overweening</td><td>/ˌoʊvərˈwiːnɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>傲慢的，专横的</td><td>overweening pride<br>过度的骄傲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His overweening confidence led him to ignore every warning from the engineers.  他过度的自信使他忽视了工程师的每一次警告。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 plethoric</del></td><td>/pləˈθɔrɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>过多的，过剩的</td><td>a plethoric supply<br>过剩的供应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The market suffers from a plethoric supply of cheap steel.  市场因廉价钢材供应过剩而受困。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unmerciful</td><td>/ʌnˈmɜːrsɪfəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>过分的，无节制的；无情的</td><td>unmerciful criticism<br>无情的批评</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He works at an unmerciful pace that exhausts everyone around him.  他以过分的节奏工作，把身边所有人都累垮。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 frugal</td><td>/ˈfruːɡl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>节俭的；节省的；朴素的</td><td>a frugal lifestyle<br>节俭的生活方式</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They led a frugal life and saved almost every coin.  他们过着节俭的生活，几乎每一分钱都存起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 conserving</td><td>/kənˈsɜːrvɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>节约的，保存的</td><td>conserving energy<br>节约能源</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Simple habits like conserving water reduce the monthly bills.  节约用水这类简单习惯能降低每月账单。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 economical</td><td>/ˌekəˈnɑːmɪkəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>节约的，经济的</td><td>an economical car<br>省油的汽车</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A small diesel engine is more economical on long journeys.  小型柴油发动机长途出行更省油。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 penny-pinching</td><td>/ˈpɛni ˌpɪntʃɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>吝啬的，抠门的</td><td>penny-pinching habits<br>抠门的习惯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His penny-pinching ways annoyed everyone at the restaurant.  他抠门的做法让餐馆里所有人都不快。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 scrimping</td><td>/ˈskrɪmpɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>省吃俭用的，节省的</td><td>scrimping on food<br>在吃上省钱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After years of scrimping, they finally bought a small house.  省吃俭用多年后，他们终于买了一栋小房子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 skimping</td><td>/ˈskɪmpɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>克扣的，偷工减料的</td><td>skimping on materials<br>在材料上偷工减料</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Skimping on safety checks eventually caused the accident.  在安全检查上偷工减料最终导致了事故。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 thrifty</td><td>/ˈθrɪfti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>节俭的</td><td>a thrifty shopper<br>节俭的购物者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Thrifty habits helped her pay off the loan early.  节俭的习惯帮她提前还清了贷款。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 middling</td><td>/ˈmɪdlɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>中等的，一般的</td><td>middling quality<br>中等的质量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The restaurant serves middling food at prices that feel a bit high.  这家餐厅食物一般，价格却偏高。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 moderate</td><td>/ˈmɑːdəreɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词、形容词</td><td>缓和，减轻；适度的</td><td>moderate one's demands<br>降低自己的要求</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He agreed to moderate his tone during the debate.  他同意在辩论中缓和语气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 modest</td><td>/ˈmɑːdəst/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>谦虚的；不大的，适度的</td><td>modest about her success<br>对成功很谦虚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He stayed modest about his award and thanked his team.  他对自己的奖项很谦虚，还感谢了团队。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 reasonable</td><td>/ˈriːzənəbəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>合理的，通情达理的</td><td>a reasonable request<br>合理的请求</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That seems like a perfectly reasonable request.  那似乎是完全合理的要求。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 temperate</td><td>/ˈtempərət/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>（言行举止）有分寸的，有节制的</td><td>a temperate response<br>有分寸的回应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave a temperate reply to a very rude question.  面对无礼的问题她的回答很有分寸。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>extricate</b></td><td>/ˈekstrɪkeɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使解脱，救出</td><td>extricate himself from debt<br>使自己脱离债务</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It took him years to extricate himself from serious debt.  他花了好几年才使自己脱离沉重债务。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disengage</td><td>/ˌdɪsɪnˈɡeɪdʒ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词、不及物动词</td><td>分开，使脱离</td><td>disengage from the conflict<br>从冲突中脱离</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Both armies agreed to disengage from the conflict along the northern border.  双方军队同意从北部边境的冲突中脱离。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disentangle</td><td>/ˌdɪsɪnˈtæŋɡəl/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>解开，理清，使脱离</td><td>disentangle the wires<br>把电线理清</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It took hours to disentangle fact from rumor in the report.  在这份报告中把事实与谣言分开花了好几个小时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 free</td><td>/ˈfriː/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、形容词</td><td>释放，解放；自由的</td><td>free the hostages<br>释放人质</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Negotiators finally persuaded the gunmen to free the hostages.  谈判者最终说服枪手释放了人质。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 liberate</td><td>/ˈlɪbəreɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>解放，释放</td><td>liberate the city<br>解放这座城市</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Allied troops liberated the city in the spring of that year.  盟军在那年春天解放了这座城市。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 release</td><td>/riˈliːs/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>释放，解除束缚；发布</td><td>release the prisoners<br>释放囚犯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The court ordered officials to release him from custody at once.  法庭命令官员立即将他从拘留中释放。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 untangle</td><td>/ʌnˈtæŋɡəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>解开纠结，理清</td><td>untangle the knot<br>解开这个结</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The auditors are still trying to untangle the company's finances.  审计人员仍在努力理清这家公司的财务。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 enmesh</del></td><td>/ɪnˈmeʃ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使卷入，使陷入</td><td>enmesh in a scandal<br>使卷入丑闻</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He became enmeshed in a lawsuit that lasted a decade.  他被卷入一场持续十年的诉讼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 entangle</td><td>/ɪnˈtæŋɡl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使缠住，使卷入；使复杂</td><td>entangle the negotiations<br>使谈判变复杂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 New objections entangled the negotiations and delayed the deal for weeks.  新的反对意见使谈判陷入纠缠，把交易推迟了数周。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 embroil</td><td>/emˈbrɔɪl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使卷入纠纷，使陷入</td><td>embroil him in a lawsuit<br>使他陷入诉讼</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The memo embroiled the whole department in a bitter dispute.  那份备忘录把整个部门卷入了激烈的争执。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>exuberant</b></td><td>/ɪɡˈzuːbərənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>热情洋溢的；繁茂的</td><td>exuberant applause from fans<br>粉丝热情的掌声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Exuberant applause from fans greeted the singer's surprise return.  粉丝们热情洋溢的掌声迎接歌手的意外回归。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ebullient</td><td>/ɪˈbʌliənt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>热情洋溢的，兴高采烈的</td><td>an ebullient host<br>热情洋溢的主人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An ebullient host greeted every guest at the door with genuine delight.  热情洋溢的主人在门口由衷欢喜地迎接每位客人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 effervescent</td><td>/ˌefərˈvesənt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>热情活泼的；起泡的</td><td>an effervescent personality<br>活泼热情的性格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her effervescent laughter filled the small crowded kitchen.  她欢快的笑声充满了狭小拥挤的厨房。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 frolic</td><td>/ˈfrɑːlɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>嬉戏，欢闹</td><td>frolic in the snow<br>在雪中嬉戏</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The puppies frolicked in the garden all afternoon.  小狗们整个下午都在花园里嬉戏。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 buoyant</td><td>/ˈbuːjənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>心情好的</td><td>in a buoyant mood<br>心情愉快</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He arrived in a buoyant mood after hearing the good news.  听到好消息后，他心情愉快地到了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bouncy</td><td>/ˈbaʊnsi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有弹性的；活泼的</td><td>a bouncy rubber ball<br>弹性很好的橡皮球</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children loved the bouncy mattress in the guest room.  孩子们很喜欢客房里那张弹性十足的床垫。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vivacious</td><td>/vɪˈveɪʃəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>活泼的，快活的</td><td>a vivacious personality<br>活泼的性格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her vivacious manner made every stranger feel welcome at the table.  她活泼的举止让每个陌生人在餐桌旁都感到受欢迎。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 austere</td><td>/ɔːˈstɪr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>朴素的，朴实无华的</td><td>an austere room<br>简朴的房间</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He slept in an austere room with a bed and one chair.  他睡在一间只有一张床和一把椅子的朴素房间里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>exude</b></td><td>/ɪɡˈzuːd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>散发；渗出；流露</td><td>exude confidence<br>散发出自信</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new captain exudes confidence whenever the game gets tight.  每当比赛紧张，这位新队长都散发出自信。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bleed</td><td>/ˈbliːd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>流血；渗出，渗色</td><td>bleed through the bandage<br>渗过绷带</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The red dye bled onto the white shirt in the wash.  洗涤时红色染料渗到了白衬衫上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ooze</td><td>/ˈuːz/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>渗出，慢慢流出</td><td>ooze out of the wound<br>从伤口渗出</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Thick sap oozed slowly from the cut in the bark.  浓稠的树液从树皮的切口慢慢渗出。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 seep</td><td>/ˈsiːp/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>渗漏，渗透</td><td>seep through the ceiling<br>渗过天花板</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rainwater seeped into the basement through a narrow crack.  雨水从一道窄缝渗进了地下室。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 absorb</td><td>/əbˈzɔːrb/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使全神贯注；吸收</td><td>be absorbed in the book<br>沉浸在书里</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The puzzle absorbed him completely for the whole afternoon.  这道谜题让他整个下午都全神贯注。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 10

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>exult</b></td><td>/ɪɡˈzʌlt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>感到欢喜</td><td>exult in her victory<br>为她的胜利而欢喜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The fans exulted in the victory long after the final whistle.  终场哨响很久之后球迷们仍在为胜利欢喜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 delight</td><td>/dɪˈlaɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>使高兴，使愉快；愉悦</td><td>delight the audience<br>使观众高兴</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The young pianist delighted the audience with a lively encore.  这位年轻钢琴家用一首活泼的加演曲取悦了观众。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 glory</td><td>/ˈɡlɔːri/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>感到自豪，得意；荣耀</td><td>glory in her success<br>为她的成功而得意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He gloried in the praise his research finally received.  他为研究终于获得的赞誉而洋洋自得。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 jubilate</td><td>/ˈdʒubəleɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>欢欣，喜悦</td><td>jubilate over the news<br>为这消息而欢欣</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The whole town began to jubilate when the rescue team returned safely.  救援队平安归来时，全城开始欢庆。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 joy</td><td>/ˈdʒɔɪ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>欢乐，喜悦</td><td>jump for joy<br>高兴得跳起来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The news of his recovery filled the whole family with joy.  他康复的消息让全家人充满喜悦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rejoice</td><td>/rɪˈdʒɔɪs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>欣喜，喜悦</td><td>rejoice at the news<br>为消息而欣喜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The whole village rejoiced at the news of the safe return.  听到平安归来的消息，全村都欣喜不已。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 triumph</td><td>/ˈtraɪəmf/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、不及物动词</td><td>巨大成功，胜利</td><td>a stunning military triumph<br>惊人的军事胜利</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new bridge is a triumph of modern engineering and careful planning.  这座新桥是现代工程与周密规划的巨大成功。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 lament</td><td>/ləˈment/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>为…哀悼，表达痛苦或遗憾</td><td>lament the loss<br>哀叹损失</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Older residents still lament the loss of the orchards that once surrounded town.  年长的居民仍在哀叹曾环绕小镇的果园的消失。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 mourn</td><td>/ˈmɔːrn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>哀悼，悲伤</td><td>mourn the dead<br>哀悼死者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The whole village mourned the fishermen lost at sea.  全村哀悼在海上失踪的渔民。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>fable</b></td><td>/ˈfeɪbl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>寓言；虚构的故事，谎言</td><td>an ancient fable<br>一则古老的寓言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An ancient fable says the mountain spring is a mother's tears.  一个古老的寓言说，这眼山泉是一位母亲的眼泪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 legend</td><td>/ˈledʒənd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>传说，传奇；传奇人物</td><td>a local legend<br>当地的传说</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 According to legend, a dragon once guarded this valley.  据传说，曾有一条龙守卫这座山谷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mythos</td><td>/ˈmaɪθɑːs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>神话体系，传说</td><td>the national mythos<br>民族神话体系</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The film borrows heavily from Norse mythos and folklore.  这部电影大量借用北欧神话体系和民间传说。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tale</td><td>/ˈteɪl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>故事，传说</td><td>a folk tale<br>民间故事</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Grandmother told us a tale about a talking fox.  祖母给我们讲了一个会说话的狐狸的故事。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fabrication</td><td>/ˌfæbrɪˈkeɪʃən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>谎言，捏造；制造</td><td>a complete fabrication<br>彻头彻尾的谎言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The witness's story turned out to be a fabrication.  证人的说法最后被证明是捏造。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 falsehood</td><td>/ˈfɔːlshʊd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>谎言</td><td>spread a deliberate falsehood<br>散布故意的谎言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He repeated the falsehood so often that people believed it.  他把这个谎言说了太多次，人们竟然信了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lie</td><td>/ˈlaɪ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>谎言；说谎</td><td>tell a lie<br>说谎</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He told an obvious lie about where he had been.  他就自己去过哪里说了个明显的谎。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mendacity</td><td>/menˈdæsəti/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>谎言，虚假，不诚实</td><td>shameless mendacity<br>无耻的谎言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Voters grew tired of the candidate's constant mendacity.  选民厌倦了这位候选人不断的谎言。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 prevarication</td><td>/prɪˌverɪˈkeɪʃən/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>支吾其词，撒谎</td><td>endless prevarication<br>无休止的支吾其词</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her answer was pure prevarication rather than an honest denial.  她的回答纯属支吾，而非诚实的否认。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 story</td><td>/ˈstɔːri/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>说法，编造的故事，传言</td><td>make up a story<br>编造一套说法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old story about the haunted mill was pure invention.  关于那座鬼磨坊的老传言纯属编造。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 fact</td><td>/ˈfækt/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>事实，实情</td><td>a matter of fact<br>事实问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Whether the letter existed is a question of fact, not opinion.  那封信是否存在是事实问题，不是意见问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>fabricate</b></td><td>/ˈfæbrɪkeɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>捏造</td><td>fabricate evidence<br>伪造证据</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The witness had fabricated evidence to protect his own brother.  那名证人捏造证据来保护自己的兄弟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 concoct</td><td>/kənˈkɑːkt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>捏造，编造</td><td>concoct a story<br>编造一个故事</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The prisoner concocted an elaborate story to win a lighter sentence.  囚犯编造了一个精心的故事以求减轻刑罚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 coin</td><td>/ˈkɔɪn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>创造（新词）；硬币</td><td>coin a phrase<br>创造一个说法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Scientists coined the term to describe the new particle.  科学家创造了这个术语来描述这种新粒子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 devise</td><td>/dɪˈvaɪz/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>想出，设计，策划</td><td>devise a plan<br>想出一个计划</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The team devised a clever method for testing the old samples.  团队想出了检验旧样本的巧妙方法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 forge</td><td>/fɔːrdʒ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>伪造；锻造，铸就</td><td>forge a passport<br>伪造护照</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was arrested for forging a passport and two visas.  他因伪造一本护照和两个签证而被捕。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fake</td><td>/ˈfeɪk/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词、动词</td><td>假的，伪造的；伪造品</td><td>a fake diploma<br>假文凭</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The gallery unknowingly displayed a fake for almost ten years.  这家画廊不知情地展出了一件赝品近十年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 feign</td><td>/feɪn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>制造假象，装出…的样子</td><td>feign illness<br>装病</td></tr>
